On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 20 v1.0

# The Discrimination Form of Trace-Response Transformation in Artificial Neural Networks

1\. Purpose

### 1.1  Central Question

　In artificial neural networks (program hosts): what the Trace-Responses left by signals are, how they compose a set, how this set organizes activity, and how activity, in a process determined in part by history, goes on transforming this set; which mechanisms genuinely bear historical dynamics, and which are only settings made by the designer or the action of an out-of-boundary optimizer. The thread throughout, in one sentence: what signals leave, how what is left organizes later activity, and how activity goes on transforming this set.  
　This paper takes two program hosts as representatives—Host 1: the spiking neural network SNN (s1 is the frozen program of Instance P of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, and s2 an extended version set up separately in this paper); Host 2: a 4→2 linear unit isomorphic to s1, trained by online squared-error stochastic gradient descent (the delta rule)—with the KV cache and two kinds of fast weights as comparisons for inference-time carriers, and proceeds by the seven-step research procedure (Section 4): find the Trace-Responses → the single Trace-Response → organization of the set → formation of tendency → tendency carried out as activity → activity acting again on the set → extracting the dynamics types and verification.

### 1.2  What This Paper Establishes

　(1) The load-bearing clause of the Discrimination Form: Theorem 20-3′ (Section 6). On the frozen common observation 𝒪 (the difference χ of the actual single-beat increments at the same new position), the two frozen candidates—Candidate A: the default-type STDP of the s1 law (self-referential write); Candidate B: the delta rule of a single-layer linear read-out (external-target-anchored write)—each have a closed-form forward map, and under the resolution condition they necessarily have disjoint outputs. What is to be discriminated is the kind of write law through which existing Trace-Responses transform the set; the Trace-Response set itself is given by the identification and organization criteria of *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0.  
　(2) The three premises of object delimitation (Section 3): the delimitation of signal, medium, background and Trace-Response on program hosts, and the conditional mechanism of declaring the optimizer boundary (Proposition 20-A); the five classes of write routes and the decidable definition of “external-target-locked destination” (Definitions 20-1–20-4, Theorem 20-1); the separation of the training period from the inference period (Theorem 20-5).  
　(3) Conditional mechanism theorems for the seven steps (4.4): Theorems 20-2 (the cycle-section destination of external-target-anchored writes, retention in the untouched subspace, forgetting by overlap projection, and the stable step size), 20-4 (onset and direction selection of self-referential writes), 20-5 (frozen inference and inference-time carriers), 20-6 (the exact idle-wait protocol of the law layer), 20-7 (the subsequent dynamics of rewriting), 20-8 (the maintenance window); Propositions 20-A, 20-B.  
　(4) Theoretical claims T20-1–T20-8 (Section 9), breachable predictions P20-1–P20-10 and the preferred experiment (Section 10).  
　(5) The Field Induction engineering interface from the host level to the dynamics level (Section 11): the intervention intervals given by the read-out map, three entry routes, the subsequent dynamics of rewriting, interceptability and the maintenance window; object-level law formation, training substitution, Field Induction Law Engineering and weight-space programming are registered as the scope of the Field Induction Group Form.  
　(6) Qualification adjudication is an application of the criteria of *The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0, *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* v1.0, *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0, *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0 and *The Mathematical Form of Trace-Response Set Transformation* v1.0; both the criteria and the dynamics types are taken from upstream.

### 1.3  Three Columns and Three Layers

　Three columns: A, the model column (model-mode verdicts on declared models, which may be 1, 0 or ⊥); B, the prediction column (conditional conclusions and breachable predictions for real network implementations and for the general class of artificial networks, with antecedents listed); C, the reality column (empirical certification; measurement mode gives only 1 or ⊥). On program hosts the declared program is itself the object adjudicated, and Column A can be decided exactly; statements about artificial networks in general (deep networks, hardware implementations) enter only Column B. The three columns stand apart: A is not read as B, nor B as C; ⊥ is not read as 0.  
　Three layers: the model layer (theorems and runs on declared models), the implementation evidence layer (network implementations actually run) and the duty layer (verdicts on the three duties and the master formula Fld of *The General Form of Field Concretization* v1.0). The three layers are written separately and are not inferred from one another. A verdict of 1 on a program is not extrapolated to “artificial networks are concretized in general”, nor to any neural tissue; an SNN is not a stand-in for biological tissue, nor does being a program deprive it of its standing as an object of adjudication.  
　Marks: \[proved\] a proof is given in this paper; \[verified\] finite verification by the appendix programs (a number Cxx refers to a verification item in the output of Appendix B.2; covering only the generated domain and not replacing proof); \[declared\] a declared item (frozen before adjudication); \[pending\] a derivation or piece of evidence not yet completed. Evidence tags: \[Fact\] \[Published model\] \[Mechanism hypothesis\] \[Derivation\] \[Conditional conclusion\] \[Prediction\] \[Insufficient\] \[Representational choice\]. For the counterexample labels X20-1–X20-16 see 7.2.

　Appendices A.1–A.4 are the frozen programs from the appendices of the upstream main texts (byte-for-byte identical to upstream): jhc\_core.py (Appendix A.1 of \[17\]), jxs\_core.py (Appendix A.1 of \[18\]), field\_final.py (Appendix A.4 of \[18\], byte-for-byte identical to Appendix A.1 of \[S2\]), snn\_core.py (Appendix A.1 of \[S11\]). ### 2.3  Literature and Citation Levels

　Citation levels: “per the original abstract” means that the cited facts go only as far as the abstract states. Cited facts hold only within their level and serve only as comparisons and readings; they bear no theorem or verdict of this paper. No figure numbers or numerical values are cited.  
　L1  Cohen JM, Kaur S, Li Y, Kolter JZ, Talwalkar A. Gradient Descent on Neural Networks Typically Occurs at the Edge of Stability. arXiv:2103.00065 (ICLR 2021). Per the original abstract: in practice, the largest eigenvalue of the training-loss Hessian under full-batch gradient descent often hovers just above 2/η. In this paper the stability threshold is borne by the proved single-mode condition (Theorem 20-2(4)); that paper's statement of the classical analysis is not relied on.  
　L2  Evron I, Moroshko E, Ward R, Srebro N, Soudry D. How catastrophic can catastrophic forgetting be in linear regression? arXiv:2205.09588 (COLT 2022). Per the original abstract: exact expressions and bounds for forgetting in the sequential training of overparameterized linear models; connections with alternating projections and the Kaczmarz method.  
　L3  Frémaux N, Gerstner W (2016) Neuromodulated Spike-Timing-Dependent Plasticity, and Theory of Three-Factor Learning Rules. Frontiers in Neural Circuits 9, doi 10.3389/fncir.2015.00085. Per the original abstract: a framework of three-factor learning rules based on presynaptic activity, a postsynaptic variable and a neuromodulator.  
　L4  Kirkpatrick J et al. Overcoming catastrophic forgetting in neural networks. arXiv:1612.00796. Per the arXiv abstract: identifies the weights important for old tasks and slows down learning on them.  
　L5  Schlag I, Irie K, Schmidhuber J. Linear Transformers Are Secretly Fast Weight Programmers. arXiv:2102.11174 (ICML 2021). Per the original abstract: linearized self-attention is formally equivalent to an outer-product fast weight controller with finite memory capacity; the purely additive outer product is replaced by a delta-rule-like update.  
　L6  Witnauer JE, Urcelay GP, Miller RR (2008) Reduced blocking as a result of increasing the number of blocking cues. Psychonomic Bulletin & Review 15(3):651–655. Per the original abstract: the definition of blocking; conventional conditioning theories explain it by the difference between expected and actual outcomes; with two blocking cues, blocking is reduced. It serves only as a structural comparison with the “single error write” explanation (P20-1); for the biological-side questions see U20-4.  
　L7  Zenke F, Poole B, Ganguli S (2017) Continual Learning Through Synaptic Intelligence. Proceedings of the 34th ICML, PMLR 70:3987–3995. Per the original abstract: each synapse accumulates task-relevant information over time, which is used to learn new tasks without erasing old ones.

### 2.4  Usage

　“Organization” in the ontological definition means the mode of organization of the Trace-Response set; “Group Form” refers only to the papers of this series.

3\. Three Premises of Object Delimitation, and the Declared Models  
3.1  Signal, Medium, Background and Trace-Response \[declared\]  
　(1) Correspondence table (program hosts, algorithmic level L):  
　\| Item \| What bears it in a program host \| Basis of adjudication  
　\| Medium positions \| parameters (weights); states (membrane potential v, eligibility traces x, y, recurrent hidden states, KV cache entries, fast-weight matrices, optimizer states) \| adjudicated carrier by carrier by the three-way split of Definition 2′ of \[17\], not designated in advance  
　\| Signal \| actions actually exerted on the medium from outside B: input samples (entry letters); targets, labels, teaching currents (teaching signals) \| the source lies outside B; the selection and order of the training data are signal history  
　\| Background b \| structure (connection graph, which weights exist); laws (activation, threshold, plasticity law, loss function, optimizer law); hyperparameters \| retained quantities not rewritten by the signal history within the window under discussion  
　\| Initial value θ₀ \| reference starting point (the trace-free ground state of Definition 1 of \[17\]) \| the initial value is not a change left by signals; Trace-Response = the difference between θ under the actual history and θ\_ref under the reference history (signal segments replaced by rest)  
　\| Optimizer \| the executor of the write process (reads θ, activity and targets, computes the write) \| a process in Proc\_W of \[S1\]; whether its carrier lies inside or outside B is fixed by declaration (Proposition 20-A)  
　\| Activity \| forward read-out (outputs, firing, integration drive); the error on training beats; attention distributions \| determined within the beat, not self-retained (Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\])  
　The structure, laws, hyperparameters and initial values set by the designer all belong to the background or to the reference starting point; they do not become Trace-Responses merely because they determine what the network can do. A Trace-Response is only a change that the signal history leaves on the medium and that is retained. The difference of the trained weights from their initial values is a Trace-Response candidate; the existence of the weights is not itself a Trace-Response.  
　(2) Three-way split \[verified C1c, C2b\]: in s1, with the other carriers clamped, supply cut off and at rest, the weights have a retention lower bound of 31 beats ≥ τ\_W → Trace-Response carrier; the membrane potential decays geometrically by 3/4, with complete self-retention of 14 beats (envelope tail evidence) ∈ (τ\_act, τ\_W) → activity residue; the presynaptic traces decay by 1/2, complete at 7 beats → activity residue; firing is within-beat activity (τ\_act = 2 beats, τ\_W = 18 beats). This is the verdict on retained carriers under the declared framework of this paper; it is kept apart from the reading in 8.5 of \[17\], which reads the membrane potential as activity by its within-beat role, and the two do not conflict; the existing routing of Instance P of \[S11\] is cited as is. In gradient networks the weights are Trace-Response carriers and there are no residue carriers; the accumulators of the law-layer variants change only when an optimization step with a gradient is executed, and they are write-rate components of the optimizer (the law-layer component form of D18-11 of \[18\]), not object-level laws.  
　(3) Proposition 20-A (conditional mechanism of declaring the optimizer boundary) \[proved; verified C2j\]  Let the gradient write process ρ\_opt have an effective component on Org(T) within W, with its strength determined in part by T through an actual route, and let the other duties and the actual routes be fixed under both declarations. (1) HDyn of \[S1\] quantifies existentially over Proc\_W and does not require the carrier of ρ to lie inside B: under the listed conditions, an optimizer declared outside B (B\_net) can still bear HDyn. (2) Part is borne by the forward read-out inside B conditioned on Org(T). (3) The in-boundary self-feedback type requires in addition that the same acting process be borne by a mechanism inside B with its route entirely inside B; this is proved from the actual mechanism attribution of the host: under the B\_sys declaration the computation of SGD is executed inside B by the training loop of the declared program (reading weights inside B, writing weights inside B), and the carrier marks and path origins of the event table merely encode this attribution. (4) The F-layer source registration and the available evidence channels change with the declaration: under B\_sys the target enters through the law bias of a process inside B, and within the F encoding of this paper Trace\_F = 0, Q\_F = 0; by Theorem D16-SF of \[S11\] this source 0 is not read back; the model-side source is evidenced through the external-target channel of the original host (the target enters the computation of SGD from outside B), compared with the reference history of Definition 1 of \[17\], and conjoined with Part and HDyn on the same T. Under B\_net the effective component of the optimizer's write is registered as an out-of-boundary cause. F-layer preservation of qualification extends only to the components that meet its read-back antecedents.  
　Proof: (1), (2) and (3) follow verbatim from HDyn and Part of \[S1\] and its Section 7; (4) from the formation and out-of-boundary-cause rules of Appendix A.1 of \[S2\] and D16-SF of \[S11\]. ∎  
　Verification (C2j): from the real trajectory of the original GD, compile the closed projection {z00, z10} of output 0: on each beat SGD\_zi0@t = (η − 2ηW0) − η(z00 + z10) (D12 linear law), and the first-beat component of beat 0 is entered in the table; a separate pure-read process FWD inside B bears Part. Three things are preserved: the F trajectory is identical to that of the original-mechanism GD on z00 and z10, beat by beat and position by position; the components of each beat are identical; under the legitimate control (z00 set back to 0) the law values equal the components of the original GD. The four F-layer items (Trace\_F, Part\_F, HDyn\_F, Q\_F): (0, 1, 1, 0) under B\_sys and (1, 1, 1, 1) under B\_net; in-boundary self-feedback type 1 under B\_sys and 0 under B\_net (computed from the carriers and path origins of the event table, the attribution being proved by the mechanism argument of (3)). Model evidence is reported separately: the identification of \[17\] gives signal formation, retention, Part and the Definition 6′ certificate (C2a, C2b, C2h, C2i′), listed apart from the four F items.  
　Antecedents under which the F verdicts can be read back as model verdicts (item by item): (a) closed projection—the writes at the output-0 positions read only the output-0 positions and the signal (4.2.6(5)); (b) law class—each component, with activity substituted, belongs to the D12 linear laws; (c) certification—cert\_eff = cert\_read = 1, the closure of the evidence graph being given by declaration; (d) source—F-layer source registration is 1 only for out-of-boundary pulses or out-of-boundary carrier processes, and the source 0 of B\_sys is not read back (D16-SF); (e) input—all of the above belongs only to input A:t10; for other inputs such as A2 or B, the read-in positions and target positions must be redetermined and compiled separately. If any item is missing, nothing is read back.  
　Output 1: the full two-output system has not been compiled in the F encoding of this paper. Under A:t10 the positions of output 1 fall below the ground state (the reverse half-domain); one may instead write d = W0 − w, under which the own-position depth update is η(2W0 − d₀ − d₁); extension to the whole domain and source certification under this notation are to be proved separately; the verdicts for output 1 are given by the model mode of \[17\].  
3.2  Write Routes and the Bearing of the History Condition  
　Definition 20-1 (external-target-anchored composite delta write) \[Representational choice\]  The write is a process c\_ρ,x = η·a\_x·(t − ρ(y)), where a\_x ≠ 0 is the own-position activity coefficient and t a target quantity coming from outside B; with t and a\_x ≠ 0 fixed, c\_ρ,x = 0 if and only if ρ(y) = t. The coefficient takes a signed form; the direction is fixed by the sign of η·a\_x (Theorem 20-1(4)). Reading for Host 2: η \> 0 is the positive common step size and a\_x the activation input (a\_x = 1 under coincident activation). This definition applies only to composite writes with nonzero activity coefficient and is not carried over to their algebraic splits into terms.  
　Definition 20-2 (self-referential write) \[Representational choice\]  The write component reads only activity inside B and the own-position depth, and contains no external target quantity.  
　Definition 20-3 (history dependence of the destination, reported separately) \[declared\]  For a declared repeated-signal protocol 𝒫 and a set Ω of reachable initial states: (a) the destination in the observable subspace—the cycle-section limit reading, with the signals used by 𝒫 as the readings; (b) the destination of the complete state—the cycle-section limit of all weights. Each is reported separately as “history-independent” or “history-dependent”.  
　Definition 20-4 (external-target-locked destination) \[declared\]  Under 𝒫, HDyn may hold while the destination of Definition 20-3(a) is history-independent, that limit reading being determined by the external target and the signal set. Under full rank, (b) is history-independent as well; under rank deficiency, (b) is history-dependent and must be reported separately. This definition is a property of the write law and the protocol, independent of whether the optimizer is declared inside or outside the boundary, and is not to be confused with the in-boundary self-feedback type.  
　The five classes of write routes (statement of Theorem 20-1):  
　\| Class \| Representatives \| What the write reads \| Bearing of HistCond \| Loop direction \| Destination  
　\| W0 schedule write \| perturbations on a fixed schedule; first-layer KV entry writes (fixed capacity, append-only, no overwriting) \| the beat and that beat's signal \| does not hold (sandblaster type of the Fourth Round of \[S1\]; C3b) \| — \| fixed by the schedule and the signals  
　\| W1 external-target-anchored composite delta write \| Host 2 (positive step size η \> 0, a\_x the activation input) \| activity and the external target \| the single SGD process reads the Trace-Responses through the output: effective in the non-steady-state regime (C2i′), while in the convergence regime it can be entirely subthreshold, with no action (C2i) \| negative loop (C2f) \| external-target-locked destination (Theorem 20-2); under rank deficiency the complete state is reported separately  
　\| W2 self-referential local write \| the s1 law; the competitive variant of s2; pseudo-label self-training \| own-position activity \| activity-mediated (C1n) \| positive loop on w00 in s1 (C1l) \| history-selected direction issued only under the antecedents listed in Theorem 20-4 and for the single-mode pseudo-label case  
　\| W3 third-factor write \| eligibility trace × modulatory factor \| unfolded per the actual process \| per the actual process \| not issued for the members of this paper  
　\| W4 inference-time state write \| KV, fast weights, recurrent states \| unfolded per carrier (4.3) \| per carrier \| per carrier \| per carrier  
　Theorem 20-1 (classification of write routes by history condition) \[proved\]  Let the declared host have a legitimate process-by-process decomposition, with ρ acting on T. (1) Let c\_ρ,x be an effective component table covering all legitimate states and the whole window—giving when and at which position ρ acts, zero when it is off, and the direction and magnitude. If this table depends only on (beat, that beat's signal), then HistCond(ρ, T) = 0 for every T; note that a write of fixed magnitude can still have its occurrence determined by the Trace-Responses (for example, the occurrence of PLUS in s1 is determined by the gate), and such a case does not fall under this clause. (2) If c\_ρ,x reads T through activity y = Φ\_σ(s), and there is a legitimate control changing only usable positions in T such that the same-beat total component difference exceeds ε and the action exceeds ε (the four clauses of Definition 6′ of \[17\]), then HistCond and HDyn hold; when the same acting process is borne by a mechanism inside B with its route entirely inside B, the in-boundary self-feedback type is obtained in addition. (3) If c\_ρ,x reads T only through the own-position d\_x (relaxation, decay), the same as in (2) holds, with the A branch 0 and the D branch nonzero. (4) Under the write of Definition 20-1, with t and η·a\_x fixed, Δc = −η·a\_x·Δρ: when ρ(y) is monotonically increasing in the own-position depth, η·a\_x \> 0 makes c monotonically decreasing in the own-position depth (direction product (+)(−) of Theorem 18-5(3) of \[18\]), while η·a\_x \< 0 makes c increase with it (Counterexample X20-16: η = 1, a\_x = −1, t = 1, ρ = d; as d increases from 0 to 1/2, c increases from −1 to −1/2, C2o); when η·a\_x varies with depth or state, the direction cannot be fixed from the monotonicity of ρ alone, and the total difference must be decided separately. Host 2 takes a fixed η \> 0, activation input a\_x = 1 and a fixed external target, and its direction is reverse (negative loop); the main discrimination theorem 20-3′ is carried by D3 (a\_old = a\_new = 1) and D5 (positive common step size) within this working domain and is not affected by the coefficient sign of this clause. The threshold-gated potentiation c = A₊x′\[vt ≥ θ\] of s1 has positive direction.  
　Proof: (1) The five clauses of the component table—occurrence, timing, position, strength, direction—are all independent of T (of the same type as the counter register of 8.6 of \[17\]). (2) and (3) are exactly Theorem 17-5′(1) of \[17\] and the two branches of Definition 6′. (4) With t and η·a\_x fixed, c is an affine function of ρ with slope −η·a\_x; the direction of a composition is the product of the directions. ∎  
　The two-layer standard for distinguishing “historical dynamics borne by a mechanism within the medium” from “applied by an out-of-boundary optimizer”: the bearer (whether the same acting process is borne by a mechanism inside B with its route inside B; Proposition 20-A; the attribution of the whole route must be proved) and the destination (Definitions 20-3, 20-4; a property of the write law and the protocol); neither substitutes for the other. The test of removing the external target \[verified C4c\]: under the same W (A and B alternating, no teaching), z00 of s1 increases from 0.418367 to 0.607871, while T of Host 2 stays unchanged position by position—in the declaration of Host 2 the only write gate is “execute SGD on beats with a target”, and removing the target closes this only gate; this proves only this declared instance, and removing the target cannot distinguish single-process from two-process bookkeeping (Proposition 20-B).  
3.3  Training Period and Inference Period  
　Statement of Theorem 20-5 (proof in 4.4): (1) Frozen-weight inference: if no process within W has an effective action on the weight set (plasticity off, no labels, no online updates), and the declared process inventory is complete, then HDyn(χ, T) = 0 for every T ⊆ the weight set (complete negation), and Fld cannot be witnessed by a T on the weight set; this verdict of 0 is limited to the target weight set, this window and the complete proof of no effective action, and Part and the past formation history are listed separately. Inference is the carrying-out (reading) of a frozen tendency \[verified C1q, C2i, C3a\], consistent with Proposition 6 of \[S6\]. (2) Inference-time state carriers are adjudicated separately (4.3): first-layer KV entries have HistCond = 0; on the union of the entries of a two-layer KV, HDyn = 1 of the cross-layer generative type; outer-product fast weights with λ \< 1 are of the direct-read type, and with λ = 1 this bearing vanishes; delta fast weights are of the activity-mediated type; contracting recurrent states are activity residue. (3) The training period and the inference period are two declared windows of the same program; the verdict for one window does not imply that for the other.  
3.4  Declared Models \[declared\] (frozen before adjudication; changes require a new version)  
　s1: the G\_SNN parameters of Proposition 1 of \[S11\] (4 entries, 2 LIF outputs, 8 synapses; λ = 3/4, θ = 1, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 1/2, A₊ = 1/8, A₋ = 1/16, ρ = 0, W0 = 1/4, \[0, 2\]); declared history FORM = (A + teaching current (1, 0), rest, rest) × 6, REST = rest × 8, W = beats 26–43 = (A, rest, rest, B, rest, rest) × 3; T = {w00, w10}; ε = 1/100.  
　s2 (a separate version; the verdicts of s1 are unaffected): the s1 program with optional extensions—letters A2 = (0, 1, 1, 0), C = (1, 0, 0, 0), I1 = (0, 1, 0, 0), I2 = (0, 0, 1, 0); same-beat competitive WTA (among the outputs crossing threshold, only the one with the largest drive fires; ties fire together) and global reset after firing; relaxation RELAX = ρ. Verdicts on s2 use only the model-mode certificates of Definition 6′ of \[17\], are limited to the declared version, and do not carry over the R7 overall verdict of s1.  
　Host 2, GD: isomorphic, 4 entries → 2 linear outputs y\_j = Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i, W0 = 1/4, η = 1/4; on beats with a target (suffix :t10 etc.) one step of squared-error SGD is written, and beats without a target have no write. Process individuation: ordinary single net-increment SGD is recorded, by its real process, as one process SGD\_ij = η s\_i (t\_j − y\_j) (default); two-process bookkeeping is used only for the separately built implementation GD2 (Proposition 20-B). Law-layer variants: an AdaGrad-type accumulator (step size η/(1 + acc)), a beat clock, a step clock; a pseudo-label self-training variant.  
　Inference-time carriers: a two-layer KV cache (scalar keys and values, nonnegative score-normalized attention; first-layer key = value = input embedding, second-layer key = value = first-layer attention output); outer-product fast weights (S ← λS + v kᵀ); delta fast weights (S ← S + β(v − S k)kᵀ).  
　All parameters are constructed values (dimensionless rationals), not measured values.  
4\. Unfolding the Seven-Step Research Procedure

### 4.1  Host 1: SNN (s1 and s2)

4.1.0  Program Identity \[proved; verified C0a–C0h\]  
　This paper runs s1 with an independent implementation snn\_beat and cross-checks it against the frozen snn\_core.step: over the declared history of 44 beats and 200 random histories (seed 20261105), beat-by-beat, component-by-component disagreements are 0; the trajectories of the interface SNNJH of \[17\] and the interface snn\_jxs of \[18\] are identical to it; the fingerprint 84d320db9a654be6… of the W-window event log of the frozen snn\_core is the same as the one recorded in the frozen run record of s1. Cross-checking is finite verification. Proof of step-by-step identity on all inputs: with WTA and global reset off and RELAX at its s1 value, one beat of snn\_beat corresponds process by process to snn\_core.step—(1) integration vt\_j = v\_j·λ + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + teach\_j reads a copy of the pre-beat w; (2) gate g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\]; (3) reset v\_j = 0 if g\_j, otherwise vt\_j; (4) x′ and y′ are updated before plasticity; (5) the gating conditions and magnitudes of PLUS, MINUS and RELAX, synapse by synapse; (6) clipping min(W\_MAX, max(0, ·)), RELAX reading the pre-beat value of the own position. The inputs, outputs and order of the six steps are identical one by one; hence, on the common domain of the frozen letters of s1 (A, B, AB, none of the frozen snn\_core.PAT) and the teaching encoding, the one-beat successors agree for any state and letter, and by induction they agree for any finite history in this domain. The letters C, I1, I2, A2 belong to the extended declaration of s2 (the frozen snn\_core.PAT has no such letters), and runs on s2 are given only by snn\_beat. The two interfaces are another encoding of the same six steps; the cross-check of C0g compares the weights and the full state r = (v, x, y). ∎  
4.1.1  Step 1  Find the Trace-Responses \[verified C1a, C1b, C1c\]  
　Signal: entry letters (acting on the synapses through the firing of the input neurons) and the teaching current injected into output 0 during the formation period (out-of-boundary). Medium: the weights of the 8 synapses and the states of the units. Change left: the excess of weight over baseline, z = w − W0, borne by the weights and unchanged at rest (ρ = 0). The weight candidates with oriented depth \> ε at window end are exactly T = {w00, w10}; there are no reverse candidates; the signal-formation positions are {w00, w10}; without the teaching current z00 = 0 (no formation). w20 and w30 have window-end depth 274654972791/35184372088832 \< ε and are not Trace-Responses. For the three-way split see 3.1(2). Firing that varies with the Trace-Responses does not thereby become a Trace-Response (Lemma 17-1′ of \[17\]).  
4.1.2  Step 2  The Single Trace-Response \[verified C1d–C1h′, C1u\]  
　Depth δ = z. K2 holds relative to the probe A and the graded read-out vt0 (integration drive); for the binary read-out g0, the non-ground-state participation effect of z ∈ {1/16, 1/8, 3/16, …} is 0—the Trace-Response is “hidden” from the firing reading, and a graded read-out must be declared. K3 holds for w00 ∈ {0, 1/32, …, 2} under the write condition 𝒲 (A + teaching current applied with resting residues).  
　Deepening: on output-firing beats PLUS\_ij = A₊·x′\_i (reading the presynaptic trace updated on this beat, not w); within W, z00 increases from 0.418367 to 0.607871. Shallowing component: on presynaptic-firing beats MINUS\_ij = −A₋·y′\_j; within W the most negative MINUS@w00 is −0.063492. Zero change: over 8 rest beats z is unchanged. Saturation: under the declared history h\_sat (formation and rest, then (A, rest, rest) × 40), clipping first takes effect at beat 80, and z00 stops at 7/4. Addition (s2, letter A2): with the old Trace-Responses present, output 0 fires 5 times within (A2, rest, rest) × 6, and the new position w20 has z = 0.364756 \> ε; with the old Trace-Responses erased it fires 0 times, and z20 = −0.000040. Lower bound on the number of formation pairings: 1 pairing (z\_T = 1/16) already satisfies both z\_T \> ε and a difference between the within-W firing readings of the two sides.  
　Elimination (s2, RELAX ρ): with ρ = 1/16, rest L beats after formation and then enter W—for L \< 12 the firing readings of the two sides differ, and the effective-difference witness lies in the activity-mediated A branch of PLUS (D branch 0); for 12 ≤ L \< 50 the firing readings no longer differ, every component PLUS, MINUS and RELAX at both positions of T on every beat has absolute value ≤ ε (maximum about 6.96×10⁻³; complete-inventory check C1h′), and the model process inventory is complete ⇒ HDyn = 0, while z\_T \> ε and the graded read-out vt0 still varies with T, so TraceSet and Part hold and Fld(T) = 0; for L ≥ 50, z\_T ≤ ε—the tail does not rely on scanning: rest beats have no pre- or postsynaptic firing, only z ← (1 − ρ)z, so z\_T is strictly decreasing. With ρ = 1/8, for L \< 5 beats the firing readings of both sides are 0, while RELAX gives a direct-read D-branch witness: Fld is borne by direct-read shallowing.  
4.1.3  Step 3  Organization of the Set \[verified C1i, C1i′, C1i″, C1j, C1s\]  
　(1) Frame F₁ = the end of the s2 declared history FORM + REST + (A2, rest, rest) × 6, probes A and A2, joint setting family = all nonempty subsets of the element set × depth multiples {0, 1/2, 2}. Element certificates position by position: w00, w10 and w20 each have oriented depth \> ε, are signal-formation positions relative to the reference history (rest throughout), and have a supply-cut-off self-retention lower bound ≥ τ\_W (C1i′); K1–K3 are then given position by position per Definitions 3′ and 4′ of \[17\] (C1i″): fixed single-carrier probes (w00: C; w10: I1; w20: I2), read-out vt0 (unsaturated on the positive depth domain \[W0, W\_MAX\]), the other carriers at the ground state. K1: δ = w − W0 depends only on that carrier. K2: participation effect e(w) = w − W0 = δ, so the order is the same everywhere (the probe activates only that input; resting residue v = 0, vt0 = w). K3: the write condition is “that probe + teaching 0” with resting residues; vt0 = w + 1 ≥ 1 fires, the component is A₊ − A₋ = 1/16 \> 0, clipping is monotone, and δ does not decrease. Among the common declared probes A and A2, those containing that input give the same order, with no order conflict (Lemma 17-3′ is not triggered); the position is hidden from the declared probes not containing that input (w00 from A2 and B; w10 from B; w20 from A), reported separately as a hidden position. The element certificates of the three positions hold. On this element set there are 6 hyperedges and the component {w00, w10, w20}: under A, w00 and w10 jointly participate in vt0; under A2, w10 and w20 jointly participate, and the PLUS of w20 is determined by w10 through firing gating (a history-condition hyperedge). The component is issued on F₁.  
　(2) Frame F₀ = the end of W of s1, probes A and B: the formal element set contains only T = {w00, w10}, with component {w00, w10}; w20 and w30 are not elements (Counterexample X20-8). Demonstration of the relations between candidate positions (no organization component issued): when the two candidate positions are included, at the end of W (with residues in x2 and x3) the four positions connect into one component, and after a further 8 rest beats they split into {w00, w10} and {w20, w30}—the dependence between candidate positions changes with the residue state (Theorem 17-4′(1)(5) of \[17\]).  
　(3) Same-beat competition in s2: the state of C1s is a directly assigned test state, serving only as a demonstration of gating bridging (the weights of the two outputs connected into one component through the competition gate); it is not an element certificate, and no organization component is issued.  
4.1.4  Step 4  Historical Dynamics Forms Tendency \[verified C1k, C1l\]  
　Tendency Φ\_σ (D18-6): for the two window-start states, with the Trace-Responses present and erased, on the signal class {A, B, AB, A2, rest} the firing inclination g0 differs only at A, and the graded drive vt0 differs at A, AB and A2; at B and rest they are the same—tendency is defined on the signal class and precedes activity, and identical binary activity can mask different tendencies. ψ\_x (x = w00, the rest fixed at the window start, signal A) is a monotonically nondecreasing step, jumping at d = 3/32; PLUS is monotonically nondecreasing in local firing (0 → 1/8); the activity-mediated loop direction is (+)(+) = positive loop (Theorem 18-5(3) of \[18\]). The step makes the derivative criterion of 18-5(2) inapplicable; only the direction law is issued.  
4.1.5  Step 5  Tendency Carried Out as Activity \[verified C1m\]  
　A presented at beat 26: with the Trace-Responses, vt0 = 350417/262144 ≥ 1 and output 0 fires; with T set back to baseline, vt0 = 481489/524288 \< 1 and it does not fire. The external signal and the residues v, x, y enter the read-out together (Φ4a of \[S16\]; the residue term of Φ1b).  
4.1.6  Step 6  Activity Acts Again on the Set \[verified C1n, C1o, C1q\]  
　The Definition 6′ certificate of \[17\] (model mode) gives 1: at beat 26 the process PLUS acts on w00 with component 67146313/536870912; under the control setting only w00 back to baseline, the A branch is −67146313/536870912 and the D branch 0—borne entirely by activity mediation, consistent with HDyn = 1 of Instance P of \[S11\]. Affected sets: within W, beat by beat, Aff^ε ⊆ Aff⁰ ⊆ Act⁰ ∪ Post; the beats of Aff^ε are 26, 32, 38 for w00 and w10, and 32, 38 for w20 and w30; there are 6 nonzero subthreshold components; the booked-as-effective semantics W^ε\_num differs from the original mechanism at w20 and w30 at the end of W, so the semantics must be declared. Chain: existing T → raises vt0 → firing on this beat → the backpropagation gate opens → PLUS writes back to T → the next A fires more easily; the carriers all lie inside B. With backpropagation cut or plasticity frozen, the certificate is not obtained, T is unchanged, and Part remains (holding by construction).  
4.1.7  Step 7  Dynamics Types and Verification \[verified C1p, C1r, C1t, C1v, C1v′\]  
　Routing (Theorem 17-9(2) of \[17\]): s1 belongs to 𝒦\_g of \[S11\], and its verdicts are as for Instance P. Witness that condition (a) of J₁₈ fails (C1p): the occurrence of PLUS at beat 26 is determined by T through threshold gating, and with activity substituted it does not belong to the law class D12 of \[S2\]; when encoded as “as actually occurred, constant law”, HistCond\_F = 0 and the summary is “effective difference not certified”, whereas the C1n certificate is 1. This encoding has lost the gating (a constant law has no read positions, and its functional support is empty), and the evaluator faithfully computes 0 from the law it is given; this is not an evaluator error, and it is compatible with 3.6 and E25 of \[S11\].  
　Dynamics types (\[S16\]; registration provides no transfer of theorems): Φ1a ⊗g; Φ1b; Φ4a; Φ5a; Φ3a (taking effect under h\_sat); Φ2 (s2 relaxation); Φ8 of HS type (A2 recruitment) and of CMP type (s2 WTA).  
　Destination (Theorem 20-4): on the integration branch without competition, output by output, “output j eventually fires ⟺ a\_j \> 7/16” (C1v′); on the competition branch only overall onset, the first-firing winner and sustained capture under C\_win are issued (C1v, C1v′, C1t). C1r: on the background a = 1/2 (past the onset threshold), with a prior z00 = 1/100 output 0 captures A (25 firings, w00 → 2), with a prior z01 = 1/100 the roles are swapped, and with no prior trace the outcome is symmetric—a positive example on a background past the threshold.  
4.2  Host 2: Gradient-Trained Network (isomorphic 4→2 linear units, online squared-error SGD)  
　Reason for the choice: the same structure, baseline, letters and protocol as s1, with only the write law changed (“same structure, different law”), so that the difference between the two hosts can be attributed only to the write law.  
4.2.1  Step 1 \[verified C2a, C2b\]: after formation (A:t10, rest, rest) × 6, the positive candidates are {w00, w10}; the reverse candidates are {w01, w11} (target 0 pushes the weights of output 1 below the ground state; listed separately as another type); the signal-formation positions are these four; the weights are Trace-Response carriers, and there are no residue carriers. External-target-anchored writes are naturally bidirectional.  
4.2.2  Step 2 \[verified C2c, C2d\]: K3 (𝒲 = A:t10) holds for w00 = w10 ≤ 1/2 (y0 ≤ 1); past the target, the write makes the depth shallower—the upper bound of Sedimentability Nature is set by the external target. Deepening: z00 1/8 → 63/256, approaching the external target 1/4 geometrically without crossing it; addition: A2 paired with the same target gives the new position z20 = 0.126922; shallowing: A2 paired with the conflicting target (0, 1) takes the overlap position z10 → −0.126862, and output 0 under A falls from 0.992188 to 0.619232; saturation is a fixed point approached toward the external target, not clipping.  
4.2.3  Step 3 \[verified C2e\]: E = {w00, w10, w20}, probes A and A2 (with targets): component {w00, w10, w20}—connected into one organization through the overlap position w10, the history-condition hyperedge coming from the single SGD process reading the overlap position through the output; the organization topology is the same as in s1, and the sign of the internal constraint is opposite (Theorem 20-3′).  
4.2.4  Step 4 \[verified C2f, C2g\]: Φ\_σ is a linear map; ψ\_x is affine; the net write η s (t − y) (η \> 0, activation input s ≥ 0, the positive-coefficient case of Theorem 20-1(4)) is monotonically decreasing in the local output; loop direction (+)(−) = negative loop. Stability at the set level (borne by Theorem 20-2(4)): under the single mode A, J = I − η s sᵀ, with eigenvalue 1 − 2η along (1, 1), i.e. 0 \< h(γ − L) \< 2 of 18-5 of \[18\]: η = 7/8 converges, η = 1 gives a two-cycle (\|F′\| = 1; higher order pending), η = 9/8 diverges.  
4.2.5  Step 5 \[verified C2h\]: A presented (no target): with the Trace-Responses y0 = 127/128, with T set back to baseline 191/256; without labels activity is carried out as usual, and the carrying-out of tendency is separate from writing.  
4.2.6  Step 6 \[verified C2i, C2i′, C2i″, C2j\]  
　(1) Unlabeled window: T is unchanged, there is no write process within the window, and the process inventory is complete ⇒ HDyn(T) = 0.  
　(2) Labeled window in the convergence regime, WL = (A:t10, rest, rest, B:t01, rest, rest) × 3: all SGD components at both positions of T over the whole window are checked exhaustively, beat by beat and item by item; the maximum absolute value is 1/512, with 6 nonzero subthreshold components and 0 threshold-crossing ones ⇒ no action, and HDyn(T) = 0 is a complete negation.  
　(3) Non-steady-state regime: after 1 formation pairing, rest 8 beats and then enter WL; the single SGD process has an effective write and a qualifying control, and the certificate is 1: at full-history beat 11 (window beat 0) SGD acts on w00 with component 1/16; under the control setting only w00, the A branch is 1/32 and the D branch 0—a net process can equally give a positive determination of the history condition.  
　(4) The separately built implementation GD2 (Proposition 20-B): the teaching channel TGT and the error channel ERR are two channels, executed separately at the algorithmic level and independently switchable, that contribute to a common post-state. With both channels on, the whole trajectory is identical to that of single-process GD; after three beats w00 is 15/32 with both channels on, 1 with only teaching on, and 1/32 with only error on. On GD2 the certificate for the labeled window in the convergence regime is 1: the two channels each cross threshold and partly cancel, the net difference of 1/512 being subthreshold—this is maintenance in the sense of “the actions may cancel” of \[S1\]; the +1/4 and −1/4 of the exactly fitted state are an example of complete cancellation, recorded separately.  
　(5) J₁₈: see 3.1(3). Proof of projection closure: the SGD components at the positions of output 0 read only the positions of output 0 and the signal, and the components of output 1 read only the positions of output 1. ∎  
4.2.7  Step 7 \[verified C2k, C2l, C2m, C2n\]  
　Destination: for the full-rank letters {A, B, A2, C} with η = 1/4, the ∞-norm of the cube of the linear part M of the one-cycle map is 0.996094 \< 1: the cycle-section limit is unique and independent of the initial value—an external-target-locked destination. Rank-deficient letters {A, B, A2, AB}: the limit on the observable subspace is history-independent; the complete state retains, unchanged, the initial-value component along the null-space direction n = (1, −1, 1, −1); with the training letters as readings, the two-state difference n is permanently hidden (Theorem 18-7(4) of \[18\]), and it manifests once the probe is changed to C. The current write in the non-steady state still actually depends on history (C2i′), which is kept apart from the cycle-section destination.  
　Law layer: the exact idle-wait protocol (Theorem 20-6). Self-referential target: pseudo-label t = \[y ≥ 1/2\], single mode A; the two states with initial z = ±3/10 tend to 1 and 0 respectively. Dynamics types: Φ12 goal feedback (external target); Φ8 of CMP type.  
　Verdicts (model evidence and the four F items reported separately): single process, B\_sys: in the non-steady-state labeled window HDyn = 1, the identification of \[17\] gives TraceSet, FWD gives Part, and the same acting process is borne by a mechanism inside B ⇒ Fld = 1, in-boundary self-feedback type = 1 (model mode); within the F encoding of this paper the four F items are (0, 1, 1, 0), and the source 0 is not read back. B\_net: four F items (1, 1, 1, 1), in-boundary self-feedback type 0. Labeled window in the convergence regime and unlabeled window: HDyn(T) = 0.  
4.3  Inference-Time State Carriers \[verified C3a–C3f\]  
　(1) Frozen-weight inference (s1 with plasticity off, Host 2 without labels): within W, Aff = ∅ on the weight set; Part still holds; the process inventory contains no process acting on the weights ⇒ HDyn = 0 on the weight set.  
　(2) KV cache. Declaration: the object contains only the key–value entries, not counters; fixed capacity, append-only, no overwriting, no cache-dependent repositioning; empty slots are distinguished from slots written with value zero (the embeddings are all positive, so written slots are nonzero; empty slots have score 0 and do not take part in normalization). The first-layer write content reads only the current beat's input: HistCond = 0, Fld = 0 (an append-only reading set). Two layers: with T = the union of the entries of the two layers, the certificate is 1—the process GEN2k writes the new second-layer position k2\_1 (empty slot → a usable generative component), with the bearing position {k1\_0} in the first layer: cross-layer bearing; taking only the second-layer entries as T, the certificate is not obtained, and item-by-item inspection of the law shows that the second-layer write does not read the old entries of its own layer. Dilution: the participation effect of an old entry is 1, 1/3, 1/5, 1/7, 1/9 (strictly decreasing under appends of the same value); in general there is only the upper bound sc\_i·V/S, and tending to zero additionally requires the denominator S to be unbounded and the old score and the value range to be controlled; a finite cache gives only within-domain bounds; replacement, positional bias and fixed normalization windows are adjudicated separately.  
　(3) Fast weights. Outer-product write with λ \< 1: certificate 1, witness DECAY, A branch 0, D branch 1/4—the history condition is borne by direct read through decay; with λ = 1 the certificate is not obtained and this bearing vanishes. Delta rule: certificate 1, witness DELTA, A branch 1/4, D branch 0 (activity-mediated). Closed form of the retrieval difference on overlap \[proved; verified C3f\]: after writing (k\_c, v\_c), for an old key k\_a, the delta rule gives ΔS k\_a = β(k\_c·k\_a)(v\_c − S k\_c), and the outer product gives ΔS k\_a = (λ − 1)S k\_a + (k\_c·k\_a)v\_c (obtained by substituting S′). The sign of the old-value component is determined jointly by the projection k\_c·k\_a, the component of v\_c − S k\_c along the old-value direction, and decay (C3e: delta, retrieval of a (1/2, 0) → (1/4, 1/2); outer product with λ = 1: (1, 0) → (1, 1)). L5 points out that linearized self-attention is an outer-product fast weight programmer (per the original abstract); here the two belong to different bearings of the history condition.  
　(4) Contracting recurrent hidden states are of the same type as the membrane potential of s1: activity residue.

### 4.4  Conditional Mechanism Theorems

Theorem 20-2 (external-target-anchored writes: destination, retention, forgetting and stability) \[proved; verified C2k, C2l, C4d, C2g\]  
　Let a linear unit y = θᵀs have the labeled-beat write Δθ = η s (t − θᵀs), with the protocol a periodic repetition of the letters s₁, …, s\_m and 0 \< η‖s\_i‖² \< 2; M := ∏ᵢ (I − η s\_i s\_iᵀ), V := span{s\_i}.  
　(1) When V = ℝⁿ the one-cycle map is a contraction: ‖M‖₂ \< 1, and the cycle-section limit is unique and independent of the initial state; with consistent targets it is the interpolating solution, and with inconsistent targets only cycle-section convergence is claimed—the phases within a cycle need not coincide. (2) When V ≠ ℝⁿ, the component of θ in V^⊥ is unchanged beat by beat, and the component in V converges geometrically; with letters in V as readings, a two-state difference in V^⊥ is permanently hidden, and it manifests when read by a probe with a V^⊥ component. (3) If θ\_A exactly fits task A and then only task B is trained (full row rank, consistent targets) to convergence, θ\_B∞ is the orthogonal projection of θ\_A onto {X\_Bθ = t\_B}, and the residual on A is X\_A X\_Bᵀ(X\_B X\_Bᵀ)⁻¹(t\_B − X\_Bθ\_A); there is no forgetting for any t\_B ⟺ X\_A X\_Bᵀ = 0. (4) Under the single mode s, the eigenvalue along s is 1 − η‖s‖², and \|1 − η‖s‖²\| \< 1 ⟺ 0 \< η‖s‖² \< 2.  
　Proof: P\_i := I − η s\_i s\_iᵀ is symmetric with eigenvalues 1 (on s\_i^⊥) and 1 − η‖s\_i‖² ∈ (−1, 1), so ‖P\_i v‖ ≤ ‖v‖, with equality if and only if v ⊥ s\_i; ‖Mv‖ = ‖v‖ requires equality at every factor, so v ⊥ every s\_i, and v = 0 when V = ℝⁿ; the unit sphere is compact, so ‖M‖₂ \< 1. (2) On every beat Δθ ∈ V; restricted to V, the argument is as in (1); a two-state difference Δ ∈ V^⊥ satisfies MΔ = Δ and s\_iᵀΔ = 0, which is Theorem 18-7(4) of \[18\]. (3) While B is trained the changes lie in row(X\_B), and the limit satisfies X\_Bθ = t\_B; this affine set meets θ\_A + row(X\_B) in a unique point, which is the orthogonal projection; substitute. (4) Direct computation. ∎  
　Verification: C2k (∞-norm of M³ 0.996094); C2l; C4d (after A is exactly fitted, train A2 → 0: the predicted output of A is 5/8, and 0.625000 after 60 beats; training B, which does not overlap: predicted and actual both 1); C2g. Status: elementary linear algebra; its relation to the Kaczmarz iteration and alternating projections is known (L2, per the original abstract). Stated in ontological terms: the cycle-section destination is fixed by the external target (external-target-locked destination), old Trace-Responses are retained only in the subspace that new signals do not touch, and forgetting is borne by overlap.  
Theorem 20-4 (onset and direction selection of self-referential writes) \[proved; verified C1r, C1t, C1v, C1v′, C2n\]  
　Protocol (A, rest)ⁿ, starting from v0 = v1 = 0, y0 = y1 = 0, ρ = 0, no teaching, a\_j := w\_0j + w\_1j.  
　(2′-a) Integration branch without competition and without reset through another path (s1 law): the weights are unchanged before the first firing; after the k-th A beat v\_j = a\_j(1 − λ^{2k})/(1 − λ²) \< a\_j/(1 − λ²), and equality at threshold is reached only asymptotically. Hence, output by output: output j eventually fires ⟺ a\_j \> θ(1 − λ²) = 7/16 (C1v′: all 12 cases agree).  
　(2′-b) Competition branch (WTA + global reset): only the following are issued: (i) overall onset—some output eventually fires ⟺ max\_j a\_j \> θ(1 − λ²); (ii) the first-firing winner—when a0 \> a1, only output 0 fires first, and on a strict tie a0 = a1 both outputs fire together. The output-by-output “past the threshold, eventually fires” is not issued (Counterexample X20-2). Sustained capture after the first firing is issued only under (1). When both are below threshold, the asymmetry has no effect (Counterexample X20-1).  
　(1) Sufficient condition C\_win: a0 ≥ θ \> a1, y1 = 0, A₊ ≥ 2A₋. Then on every A beat only output 0 fires; w01 and w11 stay unchanged; w00 and w10 are monotonically nondecreasing until clipping. On firing beats x′, y′ ∈ \[1, 2), and the component A₊x′ − A₋y′ ≥ A₊ − 2A₋ ≥ 0; nonnegativity gives only nondecrease, and an effective positive write is verified separately (in this protocol the steady state is x′ = y′ = 4/3, with component 1/12 \> ε, C1t).  
　(2) From a symmetric start, the two outputs fire together or stay silent together on every beat, and the symmetry is preserved forever.  
　(3) Pseudo-label self-training: single mode s, 0 \< η‖s‖² ≤ 1, t = \[y ≥ 1/2\]: y₀ ≥ 1/2 → 1, y₀ \< 1/2 → 0.  
　Proof: (2′-a) The formula above follows by induction (PLUS requires firing, MINUS requires y \> 0), and monotonicity gives the equivalence. (2′-b) Before the first firing each output integrates as in (a); on the beat of the first firing WTA takes only the largest, and ties fire together. (1) Induction: before each A beat the preceding beat is rest, and the preceding A beat had a firing followed by global reset, so v0 = v1 = 0 and vt\_j = a\_j, vt0 ≥ θ \> vt1; PLUS of output 1 requires g1, and MINUS = −A₋y1′ = 0; the component of output 0 is ≥ 0, and clipping does not make it decrease. (2) The laws and states of the two outputs are invariant under exchange, and ties fire together. (3) y\_{k+1} − t = (1 − η‖s‖²)(y\_k − t), so y tends monotonically to t without crossing 1/2. ∎  
Theorem 20-5 (frozen inference and inference-time carriers) \[proved\]  
　For the statement see 3.3. Proof: (1) HDyn of \[S1\] requires some process to act on Org(T); the process inventory is complete, and the processes acting on T within W all have zero or ineffective components, so HDyn = 0 is a complete negation (Definition 7′ of \[17\]), extending only to the target weight set and this window. (2) The first layer by Theorem 20-1(1); the cross-layer bearing by the law of GEN2 (which reads the first-layer attention output), and when only the second layer is taken, item-by-item inspection of the law shows no read of its own layer; the dilution bound e\_i = sc\_i·\|v\_i − y₋ᵢ\|/S ≤ sc\_i·V/S; for the outer product with λ \< 1, DECAY = −(1 − λ)S reads the own position directly, and with λ = 1 there is no such term and the write does not read S; delta by Theorem 20-1(2); recurrent states by Definition 2′ of \[17\]. ∎  
Theorem 20-6 (law layer: the exact idle-wait protocol) \[proved; verified C2m\]  
　Working domain: three candidates—history-controlled (the accumulator increases only when an optimization step with a gradient is executed); beat clock (+1 on every beat); step clock (+1 only when an optimization step is executed, zero-gradient steps included). The discrimination holds only within the domain of these three candidates; a legitimate write rate outside them can give a fourth pattern (X20-11: a clock that advances only on idle-wait beats gives (different, same), C2m′), which is listed separately; and matching reading patterns are classified only when the candidate domain is closed or there is independent mechanism evidence. Common comparison arm: one training step immediately followed by another. Discrimination protocols: (P1) insert an idle wait—no optimization step is executed; (P2) insert an executed zero-gradient step. Conclusion: under P1, the history-controlled candidate and the step clock match the comparison arm, while the beat clock differs; under P2, the history-controlled candidate matches the comparison arm, while the beat clock and the step clock both differ. Load-bearing antecedents (for P1 and P2 only): the weights are unchanged within the inserted segment; the clock-controlled arm crosses the step-size gate; the subsequent gradient is nonzero; the visible margin is \> ε (1/32 in this model). (P3) Equal-time blockade (in a window of the same length, one arm has real gradients) is a counterexample-type control and does not carry over the antecedents of P1 and P2: one arm has real writes within the window, so both the accumulators and the weights separate (Counterexample X20-7), and it does not discriminate the law layer.  
　Proof: under P1 and P2 the weight writes of all three candidates are zero within the inserted segment, so the difference in the subsequent step comes only from the write rate; the history-controlled accumulator is unchanged under both P1 and P2, the beat clock advances under both, and the step clock advances only under P2. ∎  
　What is discriminated is the write-rate component of the optimizer, not an object-level law. EWC (L4), SI (L7) and Adam serve only as candidates; their original algorithms and concrete implementations are to be verified separately (for example, if the second moment decays on zero-gradient steps, the optimizer has a step-clock component under P2).  
Theorem 20-7 (the subsequent dynamics of rewriting) \[proved; verified C8f, C8f′, C8g, C8h\]  
　Let the weights be rewritten by e at t₀ (entry routes registered separately per 11.2), after which the two arms (rewritten and not rewritten) receive the same signal history.  
　(1) General difference formula for Candidate A (s1 law): with the same input s and the same presynaptic trace, the difference of the actual unclipped updates at position (i, j) is Δc\_ij = A₊x′\_i·Δg\_j − A₋s\_i·(DEC·Δy\_j + Δg\_j) − ρ·Δw\_ij (Δy\_j being the pre-beat postsynaptic trace difference, Δg\_j the gate difference on that beat, and Δw\_ij the pre-beat weight difference; C8f′: 0 violations over 400 random pairs of states). The same-residue short form of Theorem 20-3′ applies only when Δy = 0 and there is no clipping (Counterexample X20-3). The gate sequence, the raw components and the post-processed post-state are reported separately:  
　　(a) Same gate sequence, same residue start (Δy identically 0), ρ = 0 and no effective clipping in either arm: the actual writes are identical beat by beat, and the original difference is retained position by position (C8f: two arms presented with A only; C8h: over 1022 same-gate histories, the actual increments at all 8 positions are identical beat by beat).  
　　(b) As above but with ρ \> 0: the original difference is multiplied by (1 − ρ) on every plastic beat.  
　　(c) Effective clipping: even with the same gates the difference can be erased (Counterexample X20-4); the actual post-processing of both arms must be verified.  
　　(d) Gate flips: the difference recurs beat by beat depending on the complete state; amplification across windows and recruitment are issued only under proved antecedents or for the instances run (C8f: when A2 is presented, the w20 difference is 0.053572 and the w10 difference 0.178641).  
　(2) Candidate B (delta, periodic protocol, span V, the same step size in each arm): the difference is d\_k = M^k e; P\_V d\_k tends geometrically to zero (pull-back), and P\_{V⊥} e is retained throughout; when training stops, e is retained entirely (C8g).  
　Proof: (1) PLUS = A₊x′g, MINUS = −A₋y′s, y′ = DEC·y + g; subtracting the two arms and adding the difference of RELAX gives the formula; (a) Δg = Δy = 0 is preserved by recursion, so Δc = 0, and without clipping the post-state difference = the pre-state difference; (b) Δc = −ρΔw; (c) and (d) from post-processing and recursion. (2) The one-cycle maps of both arms are θ ↦ Mθ + c, so difference ↦ M·difference, by Theorem 20-2. ∎  
　Corollary (opposite persistence, limited to the instances listed): e = (−1/8, +1/8, 0, 0) is retained unchanged on Host 2 trained only on A, and is pulled back to (−1/12, 1/12, −1/12, 0) when trained on {A, A2}; on s1 presented only with A (satisfying (a)) it is retained unchanged, and on s1 presented with A2 it is amplified and recruits w20 (instances run).  
Theorem 20-8 (Maintenance Window, Issued Only on Program Hosts) \[proved; verified C8i, C8i′, C8j, C8j′\]  
　The two comparisons are written separately and are not merged into a single gate-preservation criterion. Comparison A (before versus after the rewrite): the two arms have the same residues and input history, and their weights are the pre-rewrite and post-rewrite values respectively; the question is whether the rewrite preserves the original reading. Comparison B (the same post-rewrite weights): both arms carry the post-rewrite weights, and the comparison is only between keeping and clearing the residue; the question is whether the residue changes the post-rewrite reading.  
　(1) \[Comparison A\] Under the same residue and input history, if the rewrite involves only positions that are never read in \[t, t′) (their inputs are not activated), the reported activity in \[t, t′) is the same beat by beat. When a rewritten position is in the read set, clearing the residue does not by itself guarantee the same gate before and after the rewrite (Counterexample X20-14: s1 with frozen plasticity, probe A, all residues 0, w00 = 5/8, w10 = 1/4; w00 is increased by 1/8, the drive goes 7/8 → 1 and g0 goes 0 → 1, C8j); to prove that a rewrite preserves the original gate, a separate comparison against the pre-rewrite weights is required.  
　(2) \[Comparison B\] Isolate the rewritten channel: when positions in the read set are rewritten, record the actual residue of that channel (in this example, the integration residue v0 = 1/2 of output 0 after B); at the next probe beat, the drive of that channel satisfies “kept residue − clean start with the same post-rewrite weights” = λv0 (3/8 in this example). The comparison object is always the clean start with the same post-rewrite weights.  
　(3) \[Comparison B\] Strict clearing and subthreshold residue are reported separately. Clearing the residue gives only the clean post-rewrite reading (the post-rewrite run is the clean start); it does not give the same gate before and after the rewrite. With the post-rewrite weights fixed, for g0 under a single beat of A, the readings with a nonnegative integration residue kept and with the residue cleared differ if and only if vt\_clean \< θ ≤ vt\_clean + λv0 (v0 ≥ 0; 96 cases, 0 violations). This criterion does not depend on whether the rewritten position is in the read set: even when the rewritten position is never read by the probe, a nonnegative residue can flip the gate (Counterexample X20-15: only w20 of entry 2 is rewritten, A does not activate entry 2, the clean drive is 7/8, and with v0 = 1/4 the drive with the residue kept is 17/16, g0 0 → 1, C8j′). Hence, when the clean start is already above threshold and the residue is nonnegative, the two arms do not differ; when the clean start is not above threshold, whether the gate flips is decided by this inequality, regardless of whether the residue is below ε (Counterexample X20-5). The residue propagation bound for general multi-beat SNNs (including the propagation of the traces x, y through writes) is registered as open; maintenance conclusions are limited to the declared readings and protocols.  
　(4) Re-coordination period: on Host 2 with a single mode s (training A only), the projection of the rewrite onto s makes the read-out difference at step k equal to \|1 − η‖s‖²\|^k times the initial read-out difference (in this example (1/2)^k·1/8, back within ε in 4 steps); e = (1/8, 0, 0, 0) is not in span(s\_A), only the projection (1/16, 1/16) is pulled back, and (1/16, −1/16, 0, 0) is retained (Counterexample X20-9). With multiple modes {A, A2}, the read-out difference is s\_A·M^k e, bounded by ‖s\_A‖₁·‖M^k P\_V‖∞·‖e‖∞ (probe in V); in practice it returns within ε in 6 cycles, whereas the bound requires 10 cycles. “4 steps” belongs only to the single-mode projection.  
　(5) In frozen inference (HDyn = 0), a rewrite is only a replacement of background: the tendency changes at once, and no write process pulls it back or amplifies it; rewrites of inference-time carriers are judged separately by their write laws.  
　Proof: (1) The two arms have the same residues and inputs; integration reads only the weights of activated inputs, and PLUS and MINUS do not read weights; if the rewritten positions are not read, every quantity is the same beat by beat. When a rewritten position is read, the drive difference equals the amount changed and can cross the threshold (X20-14). (2) vt = λv + Σws; the two arms have the same weights and their v differs by v0. (3) Both arms carry the post-rewrite weights; for v0 ≥ 0, vt\_with = vt\_clean + λv0, and the gate is a threshold function, independent of which position was rewritten (X20-15). (4) The single-mode case follows from the eigenvalue of I − η s sᵀ in the direction s, and the multi-mode case from Theorem 20-2 and the invariance of P\_V. (5) From Theorem 20-5. ∎  
　Restriction: program maintenance protocols; biological sleep serves only to locate the interface, and nothing is issued for it.  
Proposition 20-B (Process Individuation and Maintenance under Dynamical Action) \[proved; verified C2i, C2i′, C2i″\]  
　(1) Ordinary single net-increment SGD is recorded, according to its real process, as one process; under sustained labeled training in the convergence regime the net components are subthreshold (at most 1/512), and when the process inventory is complete, HDyn(T) = 0; in the non-steady-state regime, when there are effective writes and qualifying controls, HDyn = 1. (2) Two-process bookkeeping is used only for the separately built implementation GD2: two channels that are executed separately at the algorithmic level, can be switched off independently and contribute to a common post-state, and for which item-by-item intervention gives different trajectories; on it, in the convergence regime, the two channels each cross the threshold and cancel, and HDyn = 1, which can be explained by the cancellation examples of Theorem 1 of \[S2\]. (3) Removing the target cannot distinguish (1) from (2): both stop at the same time. The label channel and the output channel are two reads of the same net process; it does not follow that each applies a weight action independently; an algebraic identity does not replace evidence of actual process individuation (\[S1\], Section 3). ∎  
4.5  Common Structure and Differences with the Biological Side (\[19\]) \[verified C5a, C5b\]  
　Comparison (a structural correspondence, not a transfer; transfer requires a separate proof under the correspondence conditions of \[S15\]):  
　\| Item \| \[19\] compartmentalized third-factor circuits \| Host 1 SNN \| Host 2 GD  
　\| Trace-Response carrier \| KC→MBON synaptic efficacy (depression type) \| weights (potentiation type) \| weights (bidirectional)  
　\| Third factor of the write \| DAN (partly external US, partly returned via MBON→DAN) \| the firing of the output unit itself \| the error t − y: the target t is external, y comes from the network's actual activity  
　\| Activity-mediated write \| yes \| yes (A branch) \| yes (A branch)  
　\| Direction law \| Theorem 19-4 \| the output doubles as gating = potentiation type, u \> 0 type → continued writing (C5a, 0 violations) \| the write strictly decreases (C5a, 0 violations), with the same sign as self-limitation in 19-4(1); the bearer is the error write, not a return  
　\| Overlap (actual single-beat increment of the same new position) \| Proposition 19-3: the old impedes the new \| χ ≥ 0 (Theorem 20-3′) \| χ = −ηd \< 0 (Theorem 20-3′)  
　\| Consolidation gate \| the three gates of Theorem 19-10 \| no law-layer component \| none (the AdaGrad type is a history-controlled write-rate component, with no tag gate)  
　\| Destination \| determined by the return type \| history-dependent under the antecedents listed in Theorem 20-4 \| external-target-locked  
　Common structure: in all three, the historical dynamics fall on activity-mediated writes; the direction law (18-5(3) of \[18\]) and the difference in single-beat increments on the overlap are common discriminating quantities; in all three, the organization is joined into one component through overlap. Differences: the source of the third factor (internal return, the output itself acting as gate, an error with an external target); the weight-transpose channel of backpropagation is not in the single-layer models of this paper; the consolidation law layer has no counterpart on the hosts of this paper, and engineering can construct one by D18-11 of \[18\] without any transfer being issued; the single-element orientation (depression type, potentiation type, bidirectional) makes the sign in 19-4 flip with the orientation.  
5\. Declaration of Candidate Pairs  
5.1  Frozen Working Domain 𝔇₂₀ \[declared\] (the following items are frozen before any reading is taken; they may not be selected backward from the readings to be adjudicated)  
　Host structure: 4 entries, 2 outputs, 8 weights, baseline W0 = 1/4; the letters include A2 = (0, 1, 1, 0).  
　Common observation 𝒪: input A2—old input 1 and new input 2 are activated in the same beat (coincident activation a\_old = a\_new = 1), and the other inputs are not activated; the initial-state pair σ¹ (old position w10 = W0 + d, d ≥ d\_min \> 0) and σ⁰ (w10 = W0), with all other positions (including the initial value of the new position w20) and the residues the same; the residue domain v0 ≥ 0, x\_i ∈ \[0, 2), y\_j ∈ \[0, 2), shared by both arms; the reading χ := Δw20(σ¹) − Δw20(σ⁰), where Δ is the actual post-state minus the pre-state over one beat (including all components and post-processing); resolution ε = 1/100; e is the total error of χ.  
　Treatment of the external target: Candidate A has no teaching (teaching current 0); Candidate B carries the same target t0 (any rational number).

### 5.2  Candidates and Forward Maps

　Candidate A (self-referential write): the default-type STDP of the s1 law—A₊ ≥ A₋, any ρ ≥ 0, no competition, no global reset. Forward map: when neither arm has effective clipping, χ = (A₊x′₂ − A₋)Δg, with x′₂ = x2/2 + 1 and Δg = g0(σ¹) − g0(σ⁰) ∈ {0, 1}; with clipping, χ = clamp(w20 + raw¹) − clamp(w20 + raw⁰).  
　Candidate B (external-target-anchored write): the delta rule of a single-layer linear read-out, with no post-processing. The two arms of each control pair share the same actual step size η, and η does not vary with the old position intervened on; η may be unknown and may change across trials, and all that is known is η ≥ η\_min \> 0. Forward map: χ = −η a\_new a\_old d (η is the actual step size of that pair).

### 5.3  Antecedents (Cell by Cell)

　D1 (same reading): both candidates read the same χ, at the same new position and the same beat. D2 (the two arms differ only at the old position): the other positions and the residues are the same. D3 (coincident activation): a\_old = a\_new = 1. D4 (the law of Candidate A): A₊ ≥ A₋; a strictly positive lower bound requires A₊ \> A₋, or a separately given strictly positive lower bound on A₊x′ − A₋. D5 (the step size of Candidate B): the two arms of each control pair share the same actual step size η, and η does not vary with the old position intervened on; across trials all that is known is η ≥ η\_min. If the two arms have different step sizes, the case is not in 𝔇₂₀ (Counterexample X20-10: η₀ = 1/8 and η₁ = 1/4 share the lower bound 1/8, and χ = +1/32 falls on Candidate A's side). D6 (resolution): d ≥ d\_min, and the total error of χ satisfies e \< η\_min·d\_min/2.

6\. Construction of the Distinguishing Observation and the Discrimination Theorem

### 6.1  The Distinguishing Observation

　𝒪 is as in 5.1. Three readings: the nonnegative region (χ̂ ≥ −e; Candidate A falls in this region in every case); the strictly-positive-margin region (χ ≥ A₊ − A₋ when there is no effective clipping, the gate flips and A₊ \> A₋); the no-verdict region (e ≥ η\_min·d\_min/2, or vt0 falls within the rounding error of the threshold). Reading rule: χ̂ \> −η\_min·d\_min/2 is judged Candidate A, and χ̂ \< −η\_min·d\_min/2 is judged Candidate B; the cut point and the total error limit are uniformly taken as η\_min·d\_min/2. Exact-value predictions use the actual η of the pair.  
Theorem 20-3′ (Discrimination by the Actual Single-Beat Increment of the Same New Position) \[proved; verified C4a, C4a′\]  
　(1) Candidate A: always χ ≥ 0. When neither arm has effective clipping, χ = (A₊x′₂ − A₋)Δg, so χ ∈ {0} ∪ \[A₊ − A₋, ∞); when A₊ \> A₋, a gate flip gives the strictly positive lower bound A₊ − A₋ (s1: 1/16 \> ε). In the effective-clipping region only nonnegativity is issued.  
　(2) Candidate B: when the two arms of each pair share the same actual η (D5), χ = −η a\_new a\_old d.  
　(3) Separation: for d ≥ d\_min and η ≥ η\_min, Candidate B has χ ≤ −η\_min·d\_min and Candidate A has χ ≥ 0; when e \< η\_min·d\_min/2, reading by 6.1 makes no error and the reading sets of the two sides are disjoint; otherwise the case is undecided. When the two arms are given only reading-error upper bounds e₁, e₀, e₁ + e₀ is substituted as a conservative total budget for χ; a tighter total bound is reported separately. Example: d\_min = 1/8, η\_min = 1/4, margin 1/32, e \< 1/64. In a finite-precision implementation, cases in which vt0 falls within the rounding error of the threshold θ form an undecided region on Candidate A's side.  
　Proof: The two arms have the same s\_in, and vt0 = λv0 + Σ\_i w\_i0 s\_i is larger by d in σ¹, so g0(σ¹) ≥ g0(σ⁰) (the gate is monotone in vt; output 1 does not depend on w20). At position (2, 0): PLUS = A₊x′₂·g0, MINUS = −A₋y′₀ with y′₀ = y0/2 + g0 (input 2 fires); RELAX = −ρ(w20 − W0) is the same in both arms. The difference of the pre-booking components of the two arms is A₊x′₂Δg − A₋Δg ≥ (A₊ − A₋)Δg ≥ 0 (x′₂ ≥ 1). Post-processing is monotone nondecreasing, so the difference of the post-state increments is ≥ 0; when neither arm touches the clip, it equals the pre-booking difference. Candidate B: the two arms share the same η, y0 differs between the arms by a\_old·d, c = η a\_new (t0 − y0), and the difference is −η a\_new a\_old d ≤ −η\_min·d\_min, with no post-processing; when the two arms have different step sizes, the difference is η₁a\_new(t0 − y0 − a\_old d) − η₀a\_new(t0 − y0), whose sign is indeterminate (X20-10), so D5 cannot be dropped. Separation follows from the bounds on the two sides and the reading rule. ∎  
　Verification: C4a (no-clipping region: 15360 cases, 0 violations of the closed form; gate flips: 4408 cases, minimum χ 1/16; with clipping: 3072 cases, 0 violations of the form χ \< 0); C4a′ (Candidate B: 40 cases, 0 violations); C4h (X20-10: the counterexample with different step sizes in the two arms, and the bound under shared η).  
　Note on the reading: reading only the PLUS component is a different reading (Counterexample X20-6: with resting residue and w10 = 3/4, the PLUS difference is 1/8 while the actual increment difference is 1/16); using it would require giving, for Candidate B, the same observation definition and an available evidence channel; the discriminating reading is the actual increment.

### 6.2  Both Sides Nonempty

　Candidate A's side: gate-flip cases have χ ≥ 1/16 (C4a); without a gate flip, χ = 0. Candidate B's side: always negative (C4a′).

### 6.3  Scope of Commitment

　The discrimination holds only on 𝔇₂₀ and for the two frozen candidates listed. No common sign is issued for “STDP” or “error-driven” as mechanism families: asymmetric STDP with A₋ \> A₊x′ can give χ \< 0 under a gate flip, and error writes with post-processing or nonlinear read-outs require a separate proof. Multi-beat values (C4a″: repeated A2 after the formation of A; the difference in gain at the new position at window end, SNN +0.364796, GD −0.123017) serve only as model values and as conditional prediction P20-1(c), and bear no load for the form kind.

7\. Bounds of Misjudgment and Blind Spots

### 7.1  Bounds of Misjudgment

　E20-1  Taking the declaration of the optimizer boundary as the criterion for whether there are historical dynamics; or claiming in general that the master-formula verdict is unchanged because a carrier label has been moved; or equating F-layer verdicts unconditionally with complete model verdicts. Bound: the conditional form of Proposition 20-A and read-back antecedents (a)–(e); the four F items and the model evidence are reported separately.  
　E20-2  Splitting ordinary SGD into two processes to obtain a maintenance verdict. Bound: Proposition 20-B.  
　E20-3  Reading Part from firing counts, and misjudging Part = 0 when the Trace-Response is hidden from a binary read-out. Bound: the last clause of Definition 3′ of \[17\].  
　E20-4  Putting non-element positions into the formal element set of the organization hypergraph; treating test states as elements. Bound: Definitions 4′ and 5′ of \[17\].  
　E20-5  Reading Part = 1 in frozen inference as Fld = 1 at inference time; extending the 0 verdict to the past formative history. Bound: Theorem 20-5(1).  
　E20-6  Reading the generative historical dynamics of KV as the deepening or shallowing of existing elements; borrowing first-layer entries as the in-T history condition of the second layer. Bound: Theorem 20-5(2).  
　E20-7  Reading an external-target-locked destination as the absence of historical dynamics; failing to report the complete state separately under rank deficiency. Bound: Definitions 20-3 and 20-4.  
　E20-8  Reading finite scans or multi-beat model values as universal or load-bearing. Bound: 6.3.  
　E20-9  Reading model values as conclusions about general network classes or about others' implementations. Bound: the general rules of Section 10.  
　E20-10  Reading a structural correspondence as a transfer of qualification. Bound: \[S15\].  
　E20-11  Using the PLUS component in place of the actual increment as the discriminating reading. Bound: the note on the reading in 6.1.  
　E20-12  Reading test interventions (directly setting weights) as permitted Field Induction; admitting out-of-run rewrites into duty read-back. Bound: 11.2.  
　E20-13  Inferring identical writes from identical gates while omitting residue differences and clipping. Bound: Theorem 20-7(1).  
　E20-14  Inferring from a residue below ε that a rewrite is safe. Bound: Theorem 20-8(3).  
　E20-15  Reading Candidate B from controls whose two arms have different step sizes. Bound: D5 (X20-10).  
　E20-16  Inferring from “cutting off write-back” that all write differences vanish. Bound: P20-10 (X20-12).  
　E20-17  Classifying an implementation by a matching (P1, P2) pattern while the candidate domain is not closed. Bound: Theorem 20-6 (X20-11).  
　E20-18  Merging the two comparisons—before versus after the rewrite, and the same post-rewrite weights—into a single gate-preservation criterion: inferring the same gate before and after the rewrite from clearing the residue, or inferring the same gate with the residue kept and cleared from the rewritten position not being in the read set. Bound: Theorem 20-8(1) and (3), written separately (X20-14, X20-15).  
　E20-19  Fixing the direction of a composite delta write only from the monotonicity of ρ in depth, without asking about the sign of η·a\_x and whether it is fixed. Bound: Theorem 20-1(4) (X20-16).  
7.2  Counterexamples (all exact rational constructions, with corresponding verification items in Appendix B.2)
