　X20-1  (A, rest) protocol, competition branch: a0 = 1/100 \> a1 = 0, zero residue; none of 40 presentations of A produces a firing (upper bound on the A-beat drive 4/175 \< 1)—asymmetry below the onset threshold has no effect (C1v).  
　X20-2  Competition branch: a0 = 1, a1 = 1/2 \> 7/16, C\_win satisfied; output 1 never fires—the per-output onset verdict cannot be carried over to the competition branch (C1v′).  
　X20-3  The gate still flips at the second beat after the rewrite: the same-residue short form gives 1/8, while the actual booked difference is 3/32 (Δy ≠ 0) (C8f′).  
　X20-4  Same gates, both arms touching the upper clip: original difference 1/64, difference 0 at the next beat (C8f′).  
　X20-5  Residue v0 = 1/200 \< ε: the A drives with the residue kept and from the clean start are 801/800 versus 399/400, firing 1 versus 0 (C8i).  
　X20-6  Resting residue, w10 = 3/4, the A2 gate opens: PLUS difference 1/8, actual new-position increment difference 1/16 (C4a′).  
　X20-7  Equal-time blockade: in windows of the same length, one arm has a real gradient; both the accumulator and the weights separate, with w00 at 35209/74760 and 189700963/393760920 (C2m).  
　X20-8  At the end of W in s1, the depth of w20, w30 is 274654972791/35184372088832 \< ε (C1j).  
　X20-9  The rewrite e = (1/8, 0, 0, 0) is not in span(s\_A): when only A is trained, only the projection (1/16, 1/16) is pulled back (C8i′).  
　X20-10  Candidate B with different step sizes in the two arms: η₀ = 1/8, η₁ = 1/4 (both with lower bound 1/8), new-position increments 1/16 and 3/32, and χ = +1/32 falls on Candidate A's side (C4h).  
　X20-11  A clock that advances only on idle-wait beats, in which no optimization step is executed: under the common comparison arm, an inserted idle wait and an executed zero-gradient step, w00 is 7/16, 13/32, 7/16; the pattern (different, same) lies outside the three candidates, with margin 1/32 (C2m′).  
　X20-12  ρ = 1/16, backpropagation off: the first-beat tendency difference and the gate flip are kept, PLUS and MINUS are 0 in both arms, and the directly changed w10 still has a direct-read relaxation write difference of −1/128 (C8e′).  
　X20-13  Task B consists of repeated samples with the same target: the Gram determinant is 0 and (X\_B X\_Bᵀ)⁻¹ does not exist; the pseudoinverse form (the single-row projection after removing duplicates) coincides with the iterative limit (C4d′).  
　X20-14  Before versus after the rewrite, all residues 0: s1 with frozen plasticity, probe A, w00 = 5/8, w10 = 1/4; w00 is increased by 1/8, the drive goes 7/8 → 1, g0 0 → 1 (C8j).  
　X20-15  The same post-rewrite weights, with only w20 rewritten, which probe A does not read: v0 = 1/4, clean drive 7/8, drive with the residue kept 17/16, g0 0 → 1 (C8j′).  
　X20-16  Negative coefficient in Definition 20-1: η = 1, a\_x = −1, t = 1, ρ = d; as d increases from 0 to 1/2, c increases from −1 to −1/2 (C2o).

### 7.3  Blind Spots

　Undecided region: e ≥ η\_min·d\_min/2; different step sizes in the two arms (outside D5); vt0 within the rounding error of the threshold. Outside the boundary: STDP with A₋ \> A₊, Candidate A with competition or global reset, error writes with post-processing or nonlinear read-outs, deep networks with plastic hidden layers—these are not in 𝔇₂₀, and no discrimination is issued for them.

8\. Breach Modes  
　K20-1  The antecedent of Theorem 20-1(1) holds while some HistCond is 1.  
　K20-2  The antecedent of Theorem 20-2(1) holds while the cycle-section limits of two initial states differ; the antecedent of (2) holds while the V^⊥ component changes; the antecedent of (3) holds while the residual disagrees with the closed form.  
　K20-3  Within 𝔇₂₀ (including D5: each pair shares the same actual η), Candidate A gives χ \< 0; or, with no effective clipping and a gate flip, χ \< A₊ − A₋; or Candidate B gives χ ≠ −η a a d (η the actual step size of the pair); or e \< η\_min·d\_min/2 while the reading disagrees with the true value. Controls whose two arms have different step sizes are not a breach (they are not in 𝔇₂₀).  
　K20-4  Theorem 20-4 (2′-a): a\_j ≤ θ(1 − λ²) while output j fires; (2′-b): max a\_j crosses the threshold and a0 \> a1 while the first firing is not by output 0 alone, or there is a strict tie while the first firing is not simultaneous; (1): C\_win holds while output 1 fires or a0 decreases.  
　K20-5  The antecedent of Theorem 20-5(1) holds while there is an effective action on the weight set.  
　K20-6  Theorem 20-6: for a mechanism known to belong to one of the three candidates, with the weights unchanged within the inserted segment, the history-controlled candidate differs from the common comparison arm under P1 or P2, or the step-clock candidate differs from it under P1. Patterns outside the three candidates (such as (different, same) in X20-11) are not a breach and are listed separately.  
　K20-7  Theorem 20-7(1)(a): the antecedent holds while the original difference changes; (2): the V^⊥ component changes.  
　K20-8  Theorem 20-8(1) (Comparison A: the same residue and input history): the rewritten positions are never read within the window while the reported activity differs; (3) (Comparison B: the same post-rewrite weights): the gate difference between keeping and clearing the residue disagrees with the single-beat criterion vt\_clean \< θ ≤ vt\_clean + λv0. Reading the same gate before and after the rewrite from a control with the residue cleared, or reading the same gate with the residue kept and cleared from the rewritten position not being in the read set, is not a breach but a misreading (E20-18).  
　K20-9  (Real-object layer) For the breach conditions of P20-1–P20-10 see Section 10; they breach only the corresponding prediction and its premise set, not the model theorems.  
　Not a breach: the existence of ⊥; the absence so far of experiments on general network classes; runs outside the declared constraint set; undecided cases when the error exceeds the reading bound.

9\. Theoretical Claims and Derivation Chains  
　T20-1  The bearing of historical dynamics is determined by the write law, not by the boundary declaration: under the conditions of Proposition 20-A, the same gradient write can bear HDyn whether it is declared inside or outside B; what changes with the declaration is the in-boundary self-feedback type, the F-layer source registration and the available evidence channels (derivation chain: the quantification of HDyn in \[S1\] → C2j).  
　T20-2  External-target-anchored writes lock the destination: under full rank, the cycle-section limit does not depend on the existing Trace-Responses; under rank deficiency, existing Trace-Responses are retained only in the untouched subspace; forgetting is borne by overlap (Theorem 20-2).  
　T20-3  On the actual single-beat increment of the same new position, self-referential writes (default-type STDP) give a nonnegative value and external-target-anchored writes (delta) a negative one, and the two can be separated (Theorem 20-3′).  
　T20-4  The destination of self-referential competitive writes is selected by history, but only after the onset threshold is crossed; asymmetry below the threshold has no effect (Theorem 20-4).  
　T20-5  Frozen-weight inference is the carrying-out of a frozen tendency; historical dynamics at inference time require inference-time carriers and divide, by carrier, into the cross-layer generative type, the direct-read type and the activity-mediated type (Theorem 20-5).  
　T20-6  Within the working domain of the three candidates (history control, beat clock, step clock), write-rate components can be distinguished by the two protocols of idle waiting and executed zero-gradient steps, while equal-time blockade does not distinguish them; patterns outside the domain are listed separately (Theorem 20-6, X20-11).  
　T20-7  The subsequent fate of the same rewrite is determined jointly by the write law and by “whether the rewrite is seen by the gates or readings of the signals used”: an external-target-anchored host pulls back the visible component; a self-referential host retains it when the gates are the same, the residues are the same, and there is no relaxation and no effective clipping, and under a gate flip it propagates recursively through the complete state (Theorem 20-7).  
　T20-8  On program hosts the two comparisons are written separately (Theorem 20-8): under the same residue and input history, if the rewrite involves only positions never read within the window, the reported activity is the same beat by beat. With the post-rewrite weights fixed, for g0 under a single beat of A, the readings with a nonnegative integration residue kept and with the residue cleared differ if and only if vt\_clean \< θ ≤ vt\_clean + λv0. Clearing the residue gives only the clean post-rewrite reading; to prove that a rewrite preserves the original gate, a separate comparison with the pre-rewrite weights is made. A residue below the resolution is not a safety condition.

10\. Breachable Predictions and Preferred Experiments (Column B; none tested on general network classes or on others' implementations; not written as confirmed)

### 10.0  Format and General Rules

　Each item: premise set → derivation → observable → model value → prediction → breach and exclusion. Model values have been verified by the appendix programs; predictions are statements about the listed general network classes or real implementations. A breach requires that the whole premise set hold while the prediction fails, and it excludes only the corresponding premise set and derivation chain.  
P20-1 (Preferred) Sign of the Difference in the Actual Single-Beat Increment of the Same New Position  
　Premise set: all frozen items of 𝔇₂₀; Candidate A is default-type STDP, Candidate B is the delta rule of a single-layer linear read-out. Derivation: Theorem 20-3′. Observable: χ. Model values: C4a, C4a′.  
　Prediction: (a) on SNNs implemented with the law of Candidate A (in software or on neuromorphic hardware), χ ≥ 0 when the antecedents of 𝒪 are met; the strictly positive lower bound χ ≥ A₊ − A₋ holds only when neither arm has effective clipping, the gate flips and A₊ \> A₋, and in the clipping region only nonnegativity is used; (b) on linear read-outs implemented with the law of Candidate B, with the two arms of each pair sharing the same actual step size, χ = −η a\_new a\_old d (η the actual step size of the pair; within numerical error); when η is unknown or may vary across trials and only η ≥ η\_min is known, only χ ≤ −η\_min·d\_min is predicted; (c) (multi-beat, a prediction to be tested) frozen follow-up protocols—Host 1: after FORM + REST, (A2, rest, rest) × 6, with the two arms “old Trace-Responses kept” and “all weights set back to W0, residues kept”; Host 2: formation (A:t10, rest, rest) × 6 + REST, then (A2:t10, rest, rest) × 12, with the two arms “old Trace-Responses kept” and “all weights set to W0”; the reading is the difference in the gain of the new position w20 relative to W0 at window end. The prediction is that both hosts have the same sign as in the single beat; model values are taken only from C4a″ (+0.364796, −0.123017); the finite-window lower bound is registered as U20-1.  
　Error: e is the total error of χ; when only upper bounds e₁, e₀ for the two arms are given, e₁ + e₀ is used as a conservative total budget, and a tighter total bound is reported separately; the cut point and the total error limit are uniformly taken as η\_min·d\_min/2.  
　Breach and exclusion: (a) the antecedents hold while χ \< 0 (beyond e) → excludes that the implementation agrees with the law of Candidate A; with no effective clipping and a gate flip, 0 ≤ χ \< A₊ − A₋ → excludes “no undeclared components.” (b) The antecedents hold (including the two arms sharing the actual η) while χ ≠ −η a a d → excludes that the implementation agrees with Candidate B; controls whose two arms have different step sizes are not within the antecedents (X20-10).  
　Points of discrimination from competing theories: positive transfer from hidden-layer representation learning is not this reading; asymmetric STDP does not belong to Candidate A. Structural comparison: Candidate B's side has the same form as the “single error write” explanation of blocking in conditioning (L6, per the original abstract); that paper reports that blocking weakens with two blocking cues, contrary to the additive expectation of a single error write—this serves only as a structural comparison; for the biological-side question see U20-4.  
P20-2 Historical Dynamics after Removing the External Target  
　Premise set: (a) the only write gate is “a supervised gradient step executed on labeled beats”; (b) the only write is firing-gated STDP, the net same-beat pairing component is nonnegative, and a learned pattern still makes the output fire when presented without teaching. Model values: C4c (s1 z00 0.418367 → 0.607871; Host 2 unchanged).  
　Prediction: (a) under unlabeled replay the weights do not change (only for declared instances of this kind); (b) learned Trace-Responses do not decrease; effective deepening and saturation within finitely many steps are issued only when an effective positive write lower bound and sustained firing are supplied, and in the verification they appear only on the h\_sat instance (the clip is reached at beat 80).  
　Breach and exclusion: (b) the antecedents hold while the Trace-Response decreases → excludes the absence of undeclared weakening or homeostatic scaling.  
P20-3 History Dependence of the Destination (Reported Separately)  
　Premise set: (a) external-target-anchored delta, full rank, 0 \< η‖s‖² \< 2; (a′) the same but rank-deficient; (b) competition (WTA and global reset); (c) pseudo-label single mode. Model values: C2k, C2l, C1v, C1v′, C1t, C1r, C2n.  
　Prediction: (a) different initial values train to the same cycle-section limit; (a′) the limit in the observable subspace does not depend on the initial value, and the complete state keeps the initial-value component in the untouched subspace; (b) overall onset requires max a\_j to cross the threshold, the first firing is taken by the output with the largest drive, and a strict tie fires simultaneously; asymmetry with everything below threshold has no effect; the per-output “once over the threshold, it eventually fires” is predicted only on the integration branch without competition; (c) the destination is determined by which side of 1/2 the initial reading lies on.  
　Breach and exclusion: (b) comparing only the first firing—threshold crossed, the same residue starting point and a0 \> a1 strictly, while the first firing is not by output 0 alone; or a strict tie while the first firing is not simultaneous → excludes the implementation premise “WTA takes only the maximum, and a tie fires together.”  
P20-4 Forgetting Is Overlap Projection  
　Premise set: linear read-out, frozen features, task A fitted exactly and then only task B trained to convergence; the common antecedents are that the task B labels are consistent and that the step size lies in the stable iteration domain 0 \< η‖s‖² \< 2; branch (a) further requires task B to have full row rank (Theorem 20-2(3)). Model values: C4d, C4d′.  
　Prediction: (a) the residual of task A equals X\_A X\_Bᵀ(X\_B X\_Bᵀ)⁻¹(t\_B − X\_Bθ\_A), and is zero when the row spaces are orthogonal; (a′) pseudoinverse version: only the full-row-rank antecedent is withdrawn, while label consistency and the stable step-size condition are kept; when task B contains repeated samples or correlated rows, the Gram matrix is singular (X20-13), and the converged correction is the minimum-norm correction onto the affine set {X\_Bθ = t\_B}, δ = X\_Bᵀ(X\_B X\_Bᵀ)⁺(t\_B − X\_Bθ\_A), with residual X\_A δ for A (C4d′ covers the repeated-row instance); (b) (conditional prediction) a deep network has the same form only in the regime where the frozen-feature approximation (tangent features in place of X) holds, and the prediction error includes an approximation-error term; when the approximation holds, and its error domain, are undetermined.  
P20-5 Silent Trace-Responses and Savings (SNN with Weight Relaxation)  
　Premise set: an s1-type SNN with added relaxation ρ toward baseline; the graded read-out is measurable. Model values \[verified C1h, C4f, C4g\]: at ρ = 1/16, L\_P = 12, L\_S = 30, L\_Ret = 50; within \[12, 30) a single cue does not separate the two sides, but two cues in immediate succession do (savings); within \[30, 50) only the graded read-out separates them (silent Trace-Response).  
　Prediction: after formation, a firing-gated SNN with weight relaxation passes through three phases—readable by firing, savings and silent—whose endpoints are determined by the relaxation rate, the threshold and the temporal-summation constant. The graded read-out must be read with a subthreshold probe that does not fire (the probe is a write).  
P20-6 Discrimination Protocol at the Law Layer  
　Premise set: some write-rate mechanism; P1 and P2 can be applied; the weights are unchanged within the inserted segments of P1 and P2; the clock-controlled candidates cross the step-size gate; subsequent gradients are nonzero; visible margin \> resolution. Model value: C2m.  
　Prediction: the discrimination holds only within the working domain of the three candidates (history control, beat clock, step clock): a mechanism known to belong to one of the three has a two-place (P1, P2) reading of (same, same), (different, different) or (same, different). For an unknown implementation, the two-place pattern and its error are reported first, and classification comes afterward; patterns outside the three candidates are listed separately (X20-11: a clock that advances only on idle-wait beats gives (different, same)). A matching pattern, too, leads to classification only when the candidate domain is closed (the mechanism is known to belong to one of the three) or there is independent mechanistic evidence. If an Adam-type optimizer decays its second moment on zero-gradient steps, it reads, given a closed candidate domain, as having a step-clock component; the classification of EWC and SI must be checked separately against their original algorithms and implementations.  
　Breach and exclusion: known to belong to one of the three candidates, with the antecedents of P1 and P2 holding, while the reading pattern disagrees with its candidate (beyond the margin) → excludes the declaration of that law; patterns outside the domain are not a breach.  
P20-7 Retrieval Difference on Fast-Weight Overlap  
　Derivation: the closed form of 4.3(3). Model values: C3e, C3f. Prediction: the change of the component along the old value direction is β(k\_c·k\_a)⟨v\_c − S k\_c, û\_a⟩ for delta and (λ − 1)⟨S k\_a, û\_a⟩ + (k\_c·k\_a)⟨v\_c, û\_a⟩ for the outer product; the sign is determined by the projection, the residual and the decay.  
P20-8 Participation Dilution in the KV Cache (Low Discriminating Power; Serves as a Baseline)  
　Premise set: nonnegative-score normalized attention, a fixed query, no positional bias, old scores and value range controlled, and a denominator unbounded under appending. Prediction: the participation effect of an old entry is bounded by sc\_i·V/S and tends to zero; a finite cache gives only in-domain bounds.  
P20-9 The Subsequent Fate of a Rewrite  
　Premise set: the same rewrite e, with each of the two kinds of host followed by the listed subsequent history. Model values: C8f, C8g.  
　Prediction: on networks with the law of Candidate B, the rewrite splits by span V into a pulled-back part and a retained part; on networks with the law of Candidate A, when the gate sequence and the residue starting point are the same, ρ = 0 and there is no effective clipping, the original difference is retained, and under a gate flip it propagates by the general difference formula (amplification and recruitment are predicted only for run instances of the same kind); the persistence of the same rewrite on the two kinds of host can be opposite.  
　Breach and exclusion: the antecedents of (a) hold while the original difference changes → excludes the implementation premise “PLUS and MINUS do not read the weights.”  
P20-10 Intervention Intervals from the Read-Out Map and Cutting Off Write-Back  
　Premise set: a known read-out law and pre-write residue; the positions allowed to change and the target reading are frozen; error budget e. Model values: C8a, C8d, C8e.  
　Prediction: the interval derived from the read-out map predicts whether the first-beat gate flips (beyond error e). When activity write-back is cut off (backpropagation off), the tendency difference and the activity difference (first-beat drive and firing) are kept, and the write difference at a target position not directly changed (w20 in this example) vanishes in the corresponding subsequent window—provided that the cut channel bears all sources of that position's write difference (C8e, ρ = 0). A directly changed position may have an additional direct-read relaxation write difference (X20-12: at ρ = 1/16, the difference at w10 is −1/128), which falls outside this prediction. Tendency differences, activity differences and write differences are reported separately.

### 10.1  Preferred Experiments

　★1 (P20-1): measure the sign of χ on the same common observation 𝒪, with the two arms of each pair sharing the same actual step size; report by the three readings (nonnegative region, strictly-positive-margin region, no-verdict region); test the strictly positive lower bound only when there is no effective clipping, the gate flips and A₊ \> A₋, and in the clipping region test only nonnegativity; the cut point and the total error limit are taken as η\_min·d\_min/2 (with the sum of the two arms' upper bounds as the conservative budget).  
　★2 (P20-9): retention, recursive propagation and pull-back of the same rewrite after continued running on an STDP host and on an SGD host.  
　★3 (P20-5): the three phases and their endpoints on an SNN with weight relaxation.  
　★4 (P20-6): classification of write-rate mechanisms by P1 and P2.

### 10.2  Three-Layer Registration

　Theoretical conditions: Theorems 20-1–20-8 (including 20-3′), Propositions 20-A and 20-B, Definitions 20-1–20-4. Testable predictions: P20-1–P20-10. Undetermined in reality: P20-1–P20-10 on general network classes and others' implementations; the regime of validity of the tangent-kernel approximation for deep networks; the relation between biological blocking and Candidate B.

11\. The Field Induction Engineering Interface from the Host Level to the Dynamics Level

### 11.1  Scope

　Artificial neural network hosts are controllable: Trace-Responses (weights) can be changed directly by rule-based computation, which changes the tendency distribution. Sections 11.2–11.6 give an interceptable, predictable and recomputable correspondence from the host level (changes of the Trace-Response set) to the dynamics level (changes of tendency and of subsequent writes); for the object level only the interface is registered (11.7). All evidence in this section is computational and run evidence (Column A).  
11.2  Three Entry Routes (Registered Separately) \[verified C8c\]  
　(1) Direct weight setting: rewriting weights directly during a run (init(w) and the like). This is a test intervention, not Field Induction permitted by the target field; the change at that beat is borne by no declared process (the changed positions are not in Act⁰ of that beat), and it is an out-of-run rewrite in the sense of Theorem 18-9(4) of \[18\]; it can serve as the starting point of subsequent interventions, and the subsequent dynamics are studied as usual, but the rewrite itself does not enter duty read-back.  
　(2) Carry-over of the run's initial value: the rewritten state is taken as the t₀ initial state of a new segment. In the new segment the change is not counted as in-segment signal formation (it belongs to the reference starting point of Definition 1 of \[17\]), and subsequent Trace-Responses are measured relative to it; whether the existing organization is legitimately carried over is judged separately by its source history.  
　(3) Entry through the original write law via inputs: with the legitimate input I1 declared in this paper and the teaching signal, (I1 + teach 0, rest, rest) × 2, the host's write law changes w10 by 0.132852 and w00 by 0.000078; the firing reading of A is preserved and A2 switches to firing; relative to the reference history, w10 is a signal-formation position—the change is a Trace-Response, with a complete process record. What it preserves is the firing reading of A, not the strictly balanced integration drive of A achieved by direct weight setting.  
11.3  Intervention Intervals Given by the Read-Out Map \[proved; verified C8a, C8b, C8d, C8e\]  
　Fix the target reading (output 0 fires on A), the positions allowed to change (w00, w10) and the pre-write residue (the end state of formation–rest, v0 = 0). From the read-out laws vt0(A) = λv0 + w00 + w10 and vt0(A2) = λv0 + w10 + w20: “A's firing unchanged while A2 switches from not firing to firing” ⟺ δ₁ ≥ 42799/524288 and δ₀ + δ₁ ≥ −88273/262144; exact balancing takes δ₀ = −δ₁; with implementation error e, take δ₁ ≥ distance to threshold + e and δ₀ + δ₁ ≥ −margin + 2e. Proof: the gate is a threshold function of vt, and vt is affine in δ. ∎ On 221 grid points, prediction and actual runs disagree in 0 cases (C8a).  
　Three-arm directed rewrite (C8b, direct weight setting, a test intervention): the directed arm (w00 −1/8, w10 +1/8) fires on A2 at the first beat, with first-beat components at w20 of PLUS 1/8, MINUS −67146313/1073741824, RELAX 0 and actual 67071415/1073741824; the original-state and reversed arms do not fire at the first beat, actual −37449/1073741824; followed by the same 18-beat continuation, the changes of w20 at window end are 0.418328, 0.364756 and 0.293589. The same A reading masks the tendency difference on A2, and the new position, which was not directly edited, is written differently in the subsequent run.  
　Different legitimate prior histories (C8d): on three formation–rest histories, the distances of A2 to threshold computed from the read-out map are 303/1024, 42799/524288 and 47/128; with δ₁ set to the distance to threshold, the first beat fires, and with 2⁻²⁰ less, it does not; all agree.  
　Cutting off activity write-back (C8e, ρ = 0): with backpropagation off, the first-beat tendency difference and activity difference between the directed arm and the original-state arm are kept (vt0 547025/524288 versus 481489/524288, firing 1 versus 0); the write difference at the target position w20, which was not directly changed, vanishes over the subsequent 18-beat window (both 1/4 at window end)—the write difference at that position is borne by activity write-back. Directly changed positions have an additional direct-read write (when ρ \> 0, X20-12, C8e′). Tendency differences, activity differences and write differences are reported separately.  
11.4  Three-Level Correspondence and the Subsequent Dynamics of Rewrites  
　Host level: the change Δd of the Trace-Response set. Dynamics level: the change of tendency ΔΦ\_σ (the read-out map of 11.3) and the difference of subsequent write components (Theorems 20-3′, 20-7). Object level: registered in 11.7. Subsequent dynamics: on a self-referential host (s1 law), when the gate sequence and the residue starting point are the same, ρ = 0 and there is no effective clipping, the original difference is retained; with relaxation it shallows multiplicatively; under a gate flip the difference propagates recursively through the complete state; on an external-target-anchored host (Host 2), the component seen by the training signals is pulled back, the component in the untouched subspace is retained, and when training stops, everything is retained (Theorem 20-7). The discriminating reading (Theorem 20-3′) is the single-beat form of the subsequent dynamics of rewrites.  
11.5  Interceptability \[proved; verified C8h\]  
　Reading protocol: the three-way split of \[17\] (Definition 2′), element certificates (depth, signal formation, self-retention lower bound, K1–K3) and tail evidence; Steps 1 to 3 in 4.1 and 4.2 of this paper are their execution. Learnable boundary: the fibers of D15-9 of \[S15\] and the fiber criterion of its 4.3; Theorem 17-10 of \[17\]. Ambiguity within a reading fiber is relative to the frozen family of observations; once interventions are added (new probes, settings or removals), the fibers can be repartitioned.  
　Regulatory equivalence (a corollary of Theorem 20-7(1)(a)): in a fixed time window and on a fixed input family, if two rewrites have the same gate sequence, the same residue starting point, ρ = 0 and no effective clipping, then the beat-by-beat actual write sequences of all positions are the same and the original difference is retained—what is equivalent is only the gate sequence and the write sequence in that window and on that input family; it does not follow that the graded tendencies are the same, nor that the rewrites are interchangeable in the long run. Verification: w01 += 1/64 versus no change; among the 1024 histories of the 5-beat window over the input family {A, B, A2, rest}⁵, 1022 have the same gates and identical beat-by-beat actual increments at all 8 positions; in the other 2 the gate of output 1 flips.  
11.6  Maintenance Window (Theorem 20-8) \[verified C8i, C8i′, C8j, C8j′\]  
　The two comparisons of the maintenance window are written separately and are not merged into a single gate-preservation criterion. \[Comparison A\] Whether the rewrite preserves the original reading: the comparison object is the pre-rewrite weights, with the same residue and input history in both arms; under the same residue and input history, if the rewrite involves only positions never read within the window, the reported activity is the same beat by beat; when a rewritten position is in the read set, clearing the residue does not guarantee the original gate (X20-14), and the two arms before and after the rewrite must be measured or computed. \[Comparison B\] Whether the residue changes the post-rewrite reading: the comparison object is the clean start with the same post-rewrite weights; for g0 under a single beat of A (nonnegative residue), the readings with the residue kept and cleared differ if and only if vt\_clean \< θ ≤ vt\_clean + λv0; this does not depend on whether the rewritten position is in the read set (X20-15), and “the rewritten position is not in the read set” is not used for this comparison. Clearing the residue gives only the clean post-rewrite reading; to prove that a rewrite preserves the original gate, a separate comparison with the pre-rewrite weights is made. A residue below the resolution is not a safety condition. Re-coordination period: bounded by \|1 − η‖s‖²\|^k for a single mode, and by the cycle product and the probe projection for multiple modes. Maintenance conclusions are limited to the declared readings and protocols; the residue propagation bound for general multi-beat SNNs is open (U20-10). Biological sleep serves only to locate the interface, and nothing is issued for it.  
11.7  Scope of the Field Induction Group Form (Interface Registration)  
　The following belong to the Field Induction Group Form: how Field Induction Law Engineering intercepts the cross-layer law changes of a target field, generates Field Induction laws within its own field and regulates repeatedly on that basis; a minimal Law Engineering demonstration; object-level law formation (which must first freeze a target law and its criterion, show that the starting point does not yet have qualification, continue running after regulation stops, and then check the formative history, real governance and stable re-traversal); training substitution (which must set up an additional-training baseline, with both arms using the same law-formation standard and the permitted subsequent history); weight-space programming (transforming weights by rule-based computation) and its replacement by Field Induction Law Engineering.  
　Interface format \[declared\]: entries (letters {A, B, AB, A2, C, I1, I2, rest}, teaching suffixes and target suffixes); state (s1: 16 positions, the 8 weights and v, x, y; Host 2: the 8 weights, with an accumulator or clock added for law-layer variants); testable historical dynamics (C1n, C1t, C1v, C4a, C4h, C8a, C2j); resources (under exact rational representation, the maximum denominator bit length of the state within the 18 beats of window W grows from 54 to 88, by at most +2 bits per beat, C6a; finite-window resource results are not extrapolated to qualification on other numerical back ends); verification interface (log fingerprint C0h, replay C7a, intervention switches plastic, bap, init(w), RELAX, WTA, global reset). Direct weight setting, erasure, cutting off backpropagation and freezing plasticity are test interventions, not operations permitted for engineering.

12\. Three-Column Master Table  
　\| Object and window \| A: model column \| B: prediction column \| C: reality column  
　\| s1 Instance P, W without teaching \| Fld = 1, in-boundary self-feedback type = 1 (\[S11\]; recomputed as 1 under Definition 6′ in this paper, borne by the A branch) \| P20-2(b) \| the program is itself the object judged, and its runs can be recomputed; ⊥ for others' implementations  
　\| s1, plasticity off \| T ⊆ weights: HDyn = 0, Fld = 0 (only for this window and the weight set); Part = 1 \| T20-5 \| as above  
　\| Distinguishing observation 𝒪 \| Candidate A χ ≥ 0 (≥ 1/16 with no effective clipping and a gate flip); Candidate B χ = −ηd \| P20-1 \| as above  
　\| s2 F₁ (A2 recruitment) \| element certificates for w00, w10, w20 hold; component {w00, w10, w20} \| P20-1(c) \| as above  
　\| s2 RELAX ρ = 1/16 \| L \< 12: Fld = 1 (A branch); 12 ≤ L \< 50: Fld(T) = 0; L ≥ 50: TraceSet = 0 \| P20-5 \| as above  
　\| s2 RELAX ρ = 1/8 \| L \< 5: Fld = 1 (D branch) \| P20-5 \| as above  
　\| s2 competition \| overall onset and the first-firing winner; sustained capture under C\_win (Theorem 20-4) \| P20-3(b) \| as above  
　\| Host 2, B\_sys, labeled, non-steady-state regime \| HDyn = 1, Fld = 1, in-boundary self-feedback type = 1; F layer Trace\_F = 0, not read back \| P20-1(b), P20-4 \| as above  
　\| Host 2, B\_net \| Q\_F = 1, in-boundary self-feedback type = 0 \| — \| as above  
　\| Host 2, labeled, convergence regime \| HDyn(T) = 0 (complete negation) \| — \| as above  
　\| GD2, labeled, convergence regime \| HDyn = 1 (maintenance with the two channels canceling) \| — \| as above  
　\| Host 2, unlabeled \| HDyn = 0, Fld(T) = 0; Part = 1 \| P20-2(a) \| as above  
　\| Host 2, full rank / rank-deficient \| external-target-locked destination; under rank deficiency the complete state depends on history (reported separately) \| P20-3(a)(a′) \| as above  
　\| KV first layer \| HistCond = 0, Fld = 0 \| P20-8 \| as above  
　\| Union of the two KV layers \| HDyn = 1 (cross-layer generation); second layer alone: 0 by law inspection \| P20-8 \| as above  
　\| Outer-product fast weights λ \< 1 / λ = 1 \| Fld = 1 (direct read) / HistCond = 0 \| P20-7 \| as above  
　\| Delta fast weights \| Fld = 1 (activity-mediated) \| P20-7 \| as above  
　\| Field Induction engineering interface \| intervention intervals, three routes, subsequent dynamics, interceptability, maintenance window (computational and run evidence) \| P20-9, P20-10 \| as above; nothing issued at the object level  
　General note on Column C: for general network classes and others' implementations, everything is undetermined (⊥), because these implementations have as yet no run results under the declared protocols; this is not a negative verdict.

13\. Domain of Validity  
　1\. The discrimination conclusion (Theorem 20-3′) holds only within 𝔇₂₀, for the two frozen candidates listed, and under the resolution condition and the error budget; outside the boundary and outside the candidate domain it is undecided.  
　2\. The conditional mechanism theorems each hold under their explicitly stated antecedents; finite verification covers only the generated domain of the appendix programs, and universal conclusions rest on proof.  
　3\. Columns A, B and C stand apart; model-column verdicts judge only the declared models; the prediction column consists of conditional derivations, not yet tested and not written as confirmed.  
　4\. Verdicts on s2 use only the model certificates of \[17\] and are limited to the declared version.  
　5\. The Field Induction engineering interface is issued only from the host level to the dynamics level; the object level, Law Engineering and weight-space programming belong to the Field Induction Group Form.  
　6\. Literature is cited at the abstract level and serves only for comparison.  
　7\. The maintenance window is issued only for program hosts; nothing is issued for biological sleep.

14\. Open Items  
　U20-1  A finite-window lower bound on the multi-beat cross terms of gated STDP (this lower bound is unrelated to the single-beat discrimination theorem; when multi-beat conclusions are made load-bearing, it is their antecedent).  
　U20-2  Sustained capture and basins of attraction for Theorem 20-4 outside C\_win, after the onset threshold has been crossed.  
　U20-3  Deep networks: the form of Theorems 20-2(3) and 20-3′ when hidden layers are plastic; the weight-transpose channel; the regime of validity of the tangent-kernel approximation.  
　U20-4  The relation between biological blocking and Candidate B (the non-additive blocking of L6).  
　U20-5  Classification of the original algorithms and concrete implementations of EWC, SI and Adam under Theorem 20-6.  
　U20-6  Preservation of qualification on other numerical back ends (fixed-point, floating-point).  
　U20-7  A closed form for the firing condition on the erasure side under actual plasticity.  
　U20-8  A class-by-class three-way split for general recurrent networks.  
　U20-9  Object-level effects of the Field Induction engineering interface (law formation, training substitution) and Law Engineering itself—these belong to the Field Induction Group Form.  
　U20-10  The residue propagation bound for general multi-beat SNNs (Theorem 20-8(3)).

15\. Interfaces  
　To *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0: the clauses used are limited to the definitions and theorems listed in Section 2.1; C1i″ is the execution of its Definitions 3′ and 4′ on single-carrier probes.  
　To *The Mathematical Form of Trace-Response Set Transformation* v1.0: only the clauses with antecedents listed in its scope of inheritance are used; Theorem 20-7(1) is the difference form of its write law on s1; the law layer uses its D18-11.  
　To *The Discrimination Form of Trace-Response Transformation in Compartmentalized Third-Factor Circuits* v1.0: the structural comparison of 4.5; same group, same form kind.  
　To *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* v1.0: the s1 verdicts follow its Instance P; C1p is a recomputable F-layer witness of its 3.6 and E25.  
　To *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* v1.0: any cross-host transfer requires a separate proof under its correspondence conditions.  
　To *The Mathematical Form of Field-Law Generation in Geomorphic Concretized Fields* v1.0: the discriminating reading between external-target anchoring and self-reference, and the separate reporting of the subsequent dynamics of rewrites, can serve as a comparison for non-neural hosts.  
　To the Field Induction Group Form: the scope registered in 11.7; 11.2–11.6 are its input from the host level to the dynamics level.  
　To the final paper, *The Master Map of the Field Concretization Group Form*: the complete unfolding of the concretized-field research procedure on program hosts; the method of judgment with separate windows for the training period and the inference period.

## Appendix A  Program Source

Appendix A contains all programs used in this paper. A.1–A.4 are frozen programs from the appendices of the upstream main texts, byte-for-byte identical to the main-text appendices noted; A.5 and A.6 are the programs of this paper. The six files run when placed in the same directory; each file ends with a single newline, and their SHA-256 values are given in B.1.  
A.1  jhc\_core.py  Upstream frozen program: Appendix A.1 of *The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields* v1.0 (three-way split, depth, organization hypergraph, Definition 6′ certificates and the two-branch report)

~~~python
"""Appendix program of The Adjudication Form for Identifying Concretized Fields v1.0: core library.
Python standard library only; all numerical values are fractions.Fraction. Host-model interface: X (positions), lo/hi (clipping bounds), ground (trace-free ground state),
orient (declared orientation), init, grid (gridding), act(w, s) (activity), comps(w, y, s) (list of components (process, position, value)).
  branches          two-branch report: A branch (activity swapped only) and D branch (direct read changed after the activity swap); A + D = total difference (recomputed under the unclamped mechanism).
  branches_rev      telescoping decomposition in the other order (direct read changed first, then activity swapped).
  hc_total          difference reading: some legitimate setting makes the total component difference > ε (not restricted to available positions; for contrast only).
  avail_at          bearing positions already above threshold in the pre-beat state (the numerical part of Definition 6′(B)).
  hc_status         the three values of the effective-difference certificate: effective difference certified / functional support established / effective difference not certified.
  hdyn_cert         Act and effective difference on the same beat and the same process (model mode of Definitions 6′ and 7′).
  sed               activity-mediated positive component (optional strengthening ledger); with plus, SED⁺.
  net_deep          actual net deepening (a separate ledger).
  trace_candidates  screening of Trace-Response candidates (oriented depth > ε and < −ε listed separately; not an element certificate).
  retention         self-retention: observed lower bound and complete verdict kept apart; a complete verdict requires explicit tail evidence (Definition 2′).
  triage            three-way verdict from the lower bound, completeness and the declared τ_act, τ_W; ⊥_Tail only when there is just a lower bound and it falls short of τ_W.
  org_graph_exact   exact enumeration over all declared joint settings (hyperedges included).
  org_graph_sample  bounded sampling: reports only witness edges and coverage gaps.
  basins            basins of attraction of a map on a finite chain (each basin is the set of points whose limit is that fixed point).
"""
from fractions import Fraction as Fr
from itertools import combinations
import math
ZERO = Fr(0)
FRACS = (Fr(0), Fr(1, 2), Fr(2))

def floor_grid(x, G):
    return Fr(math.floor(x * G), G)

class Host:
    name = 'host'
    grid = None
    def step(self, w, s):
        y = self.act(w, s)
        new = dict(w)
        for (_p, x, c) in self.comps(w, y, s):
            new[x] = new[x] + c
        for x in self.X:
            v = min(self.hi[x], max(self.lo[x], new[x]))
            if self.grid is not None:
                v = floor_grid(v, self.grid)
            new[x] = v
        return new

def run(host, w0, hist):
    out, w = [dict(w0)], dict(w0)
    for s in hist:
        w = host.step(w, s)
        out.append(w)
    return out

def ref_hist(host, hist):
    return ['rest' for _ in hist]

def depth(host, w, x):
    """Oriented depth (signed): a positive value is a Trace-Response in the declared orientation; a negative value is the other type, beyond the ground state."""
    return host.orient[x] * (w[x] - host.ground[x])

def trace_candidates(host, hist, eps):
    """Candidate screening (not an element certificate). Returns (trajectory, reference trajectory, positive candidates, reverse candidates, signal-formed positions)."""
    S = run(host, host.init, hist)
    R = run(host, host.init, ref_hist(host, hist))
    w, wr = S[-1], R[-1]
    pos = [x for x in host.X if depth(host, w, x) > eps]
    neg = [x for x in host.X if depth(host, w, x) < -eps]
    sig = [x for x in host.X if abs(w[x] - wr[x]) > eps]
    return S, R, pos, neg, sig

def retention(seq, eps, tail=None):
    """Self-retention (Definition 2′). Returns (lower bound, verdict); lower bound = 1 + the last t in the record with |u| > eps (0 if no point crosses the threshold).
    The verdict is 'complete' if and only if tail evidence is given and checked, and 'lower bound' otherwise, regardless of whether the last record point is above threshold:
    a last point below threshold does not rule out a later crossing during the ensuing rest. tail is one of:
      ('coverage',)                    the record already covers the whole remaining rest domain (declared; the function does not certify it);
      ('invariant set', step, band)    band lists all points of the subthreshold set (|u| ≤ eps), step is the one-step rest map;
                                       requires the last record point to lie in band and step(band) ⊆ band (checked exactly, point by point);
      ('envelope', C, lam)             |u(t_end + s)| ≤ C·lam^s (s ≥ 0) is proved, 0 ≤ lam < 1; requires C ≤ eps."""
    last = 0
    for t, v in enumerate(seq):
        if abs(v) > eps:
            last = t + 1
    if tail is None or not seq:
        return last, 'lower bound'
    kind = tail[0]
    ok = False
    if kind == 'coverage':
        ok = True
    elif kind == 'invariant set':
        step, band = tail[1], set(tail[2])
        ok = (seq[-1] in band and all(abs(u) <= eps for u in band)
              and all(step(u) in band for u in band))
    elif kind == 'envelope':
        C, lam = tail[1], tail[2]
        ok = 0 <= lam < 1 and C <= eps and abs(seq[-1]) <= C
    return last, ('complete' if ok else 'lower bound')

def triage(lb, status, tau_act, tau_W):
    """Three-way verdict for a carrier that can be written (Definition 2′): lower bound ≥ τ_W gives a Trace-Response carrier; activity and activity residue require complete self-retention;
    when there is only a lower bound and it is < τ_W, the verdict is ⊥_Tail (no verdict of activity, residue or non-Trace-Response is issued)."""
    if lb >= tau_W:
        return 'Trace-Response carrier'
    if status != 'complete':
        return '⊥_Tail'
    return 'activity' if lb <= tau_act else 'activity residue'

def setv(host, w, vals):
    v = dict(w)
    for x, val in vals.items():
        v[x] = min(host.hi[x], max(host.lo[x], val))
    return v

def setT(host, w, T, frac=Fr(0)):
    return setv(host, w, {x: host.ground[x] + frac * (w[x] - host.ground[x]) for x in T})

def comp_of(host, w, y, s, proc, x):
    tot = ZERO
    for (p, xx, c) in host.comps(w, y, s):
        if p == proc and xx == x:
            tot += c
    return tot

def total_comp(host, w, s, proc, x):
    return comp_of(host, w, host.act(w, s), s, proc, x)

def branches(host, w, w2, s, proc, x):
    """Two-branch report (the control w2 is the state after a legitimate setting). A = c(direct read of w, y(w2)) − c(direct read of w, y(w));
    D = c(direct read of w2, y(w2)) − c(direct read of w, y(w2)); A + D = total difference = c(w2) − c(w) (recomputed under the unclamped mechanism)."""
    y, y2 = host.act(w, s), host.act(w2, s)
    c0 = comp_of(host, w, y, s, proc, x)
    cA = comp_of(host, w, y2, s, proc, x)
    c2 = comp_of(host, w2, y2, s, proc, x)
    return cA - c0, c2 - cA, c2 - c0

def settings(host, w, T, fracs=FRACS, joint=True):
    """Declared family of legitimate settings: every nonempty subset of T (joint=True), or each single position and the whole of T (joint=False), times the depth multiples."""
    T = list(T)
    subs = []
    if joint:
        for k in range(1, len(T) + 1):
            subs += [list(c) for c in combinations(T, k)]
    else:
        subs = [[x] for x in T] + ([T] if len(T) > 1 else [])
    out = []
    for U in subs:
        for f in fracs:
            w2 = setT(host, w, U, f)
            if w2 != w:
                out.append((tuple(U), f, w2))
    return out

def hc_total(host, w, s, proc, x, T, eps, fracs=FRACS, joint=True):
    """Difference reading of the history condition: some legitimate setting makes the total component difference > eps. Returns (U, f, A, D, total difference) or None."""
    for (U, f, w2) in settings(host, w, T, fracs, joint):
        A, D, tot = branches(host, w, w2, s, proc, x)
        if abs(tot) > eps:
            return (U, f, A, D, tot)
    return None

def part(host, w, T, probes, eps, fracs=FRACS):
    for s in probes:
        y0 = host.act(w, s)
        for (U, f, w2) in settings(host, w, T, fracs, joint=False):
            y1 = host.act(w2, s)
            for j in y0:
                if abs(y0[j] - y1[j]) > eps:
                    return (s, j, y0[j], y1[j])
    return None

def hdyn(host, states, sigs, T, eps, fracs=FRACS):
    """For contrast: at some beat in the window, the component of process p on x ∈ T is effective, and the total component difference is effective under some legitimate setting of T (not restricted to available positions)."""
    for k, s in enumerate(sigs):
        w = states[k]
        y = host.act(w, s)
        seen = set()
        for (p, x, c) in host.comps(w, y, s):
            if x not in T or (p, x) in seen:
                continue
            seen.add((p, x))
            c = comp_of(host, w, y, s, p, x)
            if abs(c) <= eps:
                continue
            h = hc_total(host, w, s, p, x, T, eps, fracs)
            if h:
                return (k, s, p, x, c) + h
    return None

def sed(host, reach, T, eps, plus=False, fracs=FRACS):
    """Activity-mediated positive component (optional strengthening ledger): (A) the oriented component is > eps; (B) under the same setting, the total difference is effective and the A branch is effective.
    plus: additionally requires that setting the whole of T shallower by each frac < 1 makes the oriented component strictly smaller (self-reinforcement ledger; not a universal claim over all subsets)."""
    for (w, s) in reach:
        y = host.act(w, s)
        for (p, x, c) in host.comps(w, y, s):
            if x not in T:
                continue
            c = comp_of(host, w, y, s, p, x)
            if host.orient[x] * c <= eps:
                continue
            ok = False
            for (U, f, w2) in settings(host, w, T, fracs):
                A, D, tot = branches(host, w, w2, s, p, x)
                if abs(tot) > eps and abs(A) > eps:
                    ok = True
                    break
            if not ok:
                continue
            if not plus:
                return (s, p, x, c)
            good = True
            for f_ in [f_ for f_ in fracs if f_ < 1]:
                w2 = setT(host, w, T, f_)
                if not host.orient[x] * total_comp(host, w2, s, p, x) < host.orient[x] * c:
                    good = False
                    break
            if good:
                return (s, p, x, c)
    return None

def net_deep(host, w, protocol, T, eps):
    """Actual net deepening: applying the protocol increases the oriented depth of some position in T by > eps. Returns [(x, before, after)]."""
    w2 = dict(w)
    for s in protocol:
        w2 = host.step(w2, s)
    return [(x, depth(host, w, x), depth(host, w2, x)) for x in T if depth(host, w2, x) - depth(host, w, x) > eps]

def reach_pairs(host, hists):
    out = []
    for h in hists:
        S = run(host, host.init, h)
        for k, s in enumerate(h):
            out.append((S[k], s))
    return out

class UF:
    def __init__(self, items):
        self.p = {i: i for i in items}
    def f(self, i):
        while self.p[i] != i:
            self.p[i] = self.p[self.p[i]]
            i = self.p[i]
        return i
    def u(self, a, b):
        ra, rb = self.f(a), self.f(b)
        if ra != rb:
            self.p[rb] = ra

def _act_dep(host, w, w2, s, eps):
    y0, y1 = host.act(w, s), host.act(w2, s)
    return [j for j in y0 if abs(y0[j] - y1[j]) > eps]

def org_graph_exact(host, w, E, probes, eps, fracs=FRACS):
    """Exact enumeration: finds dependences over all nonempty subsets of E × depth multiples (the declared family of joint settings).
    Co-participation hyperedge: a minimal subset U that changes activity component j (a hyperedge when |U| ≥ 2; when |U| = 1, merged into the single-position dependence group of the same j).
    History-condition hyperedge: a minimal subset U that changes the total component acting on x, together with x. Returns (list of hyperedges, connected components)."""
    E = list(E)
    hyper = set()
    for s in probes:
        y0 = host.act(w, s)
        changed = {}
        for (U, f, w2) in settings(host, w, E, fracs, joint=True):
            for j in _act_dep(host, w, w2, s, eps):
                changed.setdefault(('act', j), set()).add(U)
            for (p, x, c) in host.comps(w, y0, s):
                if x not in E:
                    continue
                tot = total_comp(host, w2, s, p, x) - comp_of(host, w, y0, s, p, x)
                if abs(tot) > eps:
                    changed.setdefault(('w', p, x), set()).add(U)
        for key, Us in changed.items():
            mins = [U for U in Us if not any(set(V) < set(U) for V in Us)]
            if key[0] == 'act':
                singles = sorted({U[0] for U in mins if len(U) == 1})
                if len(singles) >= 2:
                    hyper.add(('co-participation', key[1], tuple(singles)))
                for U in mins:
                    if len(U) >= 2:
                        hyper.add(('co-participation', key[1], tuple(sorted(U))))
            else:
                x = key[2]
                for U in mins:
                    nodes = tuple(sorted(set(U) | {x}))
                    if len(nodes) >= 2:
                        hyper.add(('history condition', key[1] + '@' + x, nodes))
    uf = UF(E)
    for (_k, _n, nodes) in hyper:
        for a in nodes[1:]:
            uf.u(nodes[0], a)
    comps = {}
    for i in E:
        comps.setdefault(uf.f(i), []).append(i)
    return sorted(hyper), sorted([sorted(v) for v in comps.values()])

def org_graph_sample(host, w, E, probes, eps, tested, fracs=FRACS):
    """Bounded sampling: settings are made only on tested (a list of subsets). Returns (witness hyperedges, coverage gaps: position pairs not jointly covered by any tested subset; being covered does not mean being identified)."""
    hyper = set()
    for s in probes:
        y0 = host.act(w, s)
        for U in tested:
            for f in fracs:
                w2 = setT(host, w, U, f)
                if w2 == w:
                    continue
                for j in _act_dep(host, w, w2, s, eps):
                    hyper.add(('co-participation', j, tuple(sorted(U))))
                for (p, x, c) in host.comps(w, y0, s):
                    if x in E and abs(total_comp(host, w2, s, p, x) - comp_of(host, w, y0, s, p, x)) > eps:
                        hyper.add(('history condition', p + '@' + x, tuple(sorted(set(U) | {x}))))
    covered = set()
    for U in tested:
        for a in U:
            for b in U:
                covered.add((a, b))
    unknown = [(a, b) for a, b in combinations(sorted(E), 2) if (a, b) not in covered]
    return sorted(hyper), unknown

def basins(F, chain):
    """F (a dict) is a map on chain, a finite totally ordered chain. Returns {fixed point: [its basin of attraction (the points whose limit is that point)]}."""
    out = {}
    for d in chain:
        x, seen = d, set()
        while F[x] != x:
            if x in seen:
                x = None
                break
            seen.add(x)
            x = F[x]
        out.setdefault(x, []).append(d)
    return out

def order_rel(a1, a2):
    return (a1 > a2) - (a1 < a2)

def order_conflicts(pairs):
    """Returns the point pairs whose order relations under q and e disagree (including equality mismatches in both directions)."""
    bad = []
    for i in range(len(pairs)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(pairs)):
            (q1, e1), (q2, e2) = pairs[i], pairs[j]
            if order_rel(q1, q2) != order_rel(e1, e2):
                bad.append((pairs[i], pairs[j]))
    return bad

def fstr(x):
    if isinstance(x, Fr):
        return str(x.numerator) if x.denominator == 1 else '%d/%d' % (x.numerator, x.denominator)
    return str(x)

def avail_at(host, states, k, T, eps, k_form=None):
    """Available bearing positions at beat k: the positions in T whose oriented depth is > eps in the pre-beat state states[k] of beat k.
    states[k] results from the writes of the earlier beats, so its threshold crossing means the trace was left before beat k; if the crossing dates from states[0] (above threshold at the start),
    its formation needs a separate record (the counterpart of ENT in [S3]), which this function does not certify; here the program verifies only that the trace was numerically “already left”.
    Window-end membership in T is not used for this judgment."""
    return [x for x in T if depth(host, states[k], x) > eps]

def hc_status(host, w, s, proc, x, U_av, eps, fracs=FRACS):
    """Three values of the history condition on one beat and one process (over the family of legitimate settings that change only the available positions U_av, joint settings included):
    'effective difference certified': some setting makes the total component difference > eps; 'functional support established': some setting makes the total component difference ≠ 0 but none makes it > eps;
    'effective difference not certified': the total component difference is identically 0 over the tested setting family (this is only a reading within a finite setting family, not a complete negation)."""
    best = None
    nonzero = False
    for (U, f, w2) in settings(host, w, U_av, fracs, joint=True):
        A, D, tot = branches(host, w, w2, s, proc, x)
        if abs(tot) > eps:
            return 'effective difference certified', (U, f, A, D, tot)
        if tot != 0:
            nonzero = True
            best = (U, f, A, D, tot)
    if nonzero:
        return 'functional support established', best
    return 'effective difference not certified', None

def hdyn_cert(host, states, sigs, T, eps, fracs=FRACS):
    """Act and history difference on the same beat and the same process: Act = the total component c of that process on x ∈ T at that beat has |c| > eps;
    the settings change only the available bearing positions of that beat (avail_at). Returns (value, witness); value ∈ {'1', 'functional support established', 'not certified'}.
    This function verifies only the structural dependence and numerical difference of the model (model mode); the actual material route and action certification are left to the declaration."""
    status = 'not certified'
    wit = None
    for k, s in enumerate(sigs):
        w = states[k]
        Uav = avail_at(host, states, k, T, eps)
        if not Uav:
            continue
        y = host.act(w, s)
        seen = set()
        for (p, x, _c) in host.comps(w, y, s):
            if x not in T or (p, x) in seen:
                continue
            seen.add((p, x))
            c = comp_of(host, w, y, s, p, x)
            if abs(c) <= eps:
                continue
            st, wt = hc_status(host, w, s, p, x, Uav, eps, fracs)
            if st == 'effective difference certified':
                return '1', (k, s, p, x, c, Uav) + wt
            if st == 'functional support established' and status == 'not certified':
                status, wit = 'functional support established', (k, s, p, x, c, Uav) + wt
    return status, wit

def branches_rev(host, w, w2, s, proc, x):
    """Telescoping decomposition in the other declared order: direct read changed first (activity kept at its original value), then activity swapped. Returns (D″, A″, total difference)."""
    y, y2 = host.act(w, s), host.act(w2, s)
    c0 = comp_of(host, w, y, s, proc, x)
    cD = comp_of(host, w2, y, s, proc, x)
    c2 = comp_of(host, w2, y2, s, proc, x)
    return cD - c0, c2 - cD, c2 - c0
~~~

A.2  jxs\_core.py  Upstream frozen program: Appendix A.1 of *The Mathematical Form of Trace-Response Set Transformation* v1.0 (hosts, tendency, Act/Aff, runs and read-back, J₁₈)

~~~python
# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
"""
Appendix program of The Mathematical Form of Trace-Response Set Transformation v1.0: core library of the run system.
Corresponds to Sections 3–5 of the main text: the complete state σ = (d, r); the read-out law Φ; write components; the residue law P; the two resolution semantics;
tendency, affected set, action set; exact dynamical identity; the permanent-hiding criterion for linear time-invariant closed loops; F-layer compilation and the two readings of the history condition.
Discipline: standard library only; every numerical value is a fractions.Fraction; no random numbers.
"""
from fractions import Fraction as F
from itertools import product

class Host:
    """Declared host model 𝔐 (D18-1).
    positions: list of Structural Positions with identity labels (X̂; may include latent positions controlled by existence variables).
    read(d, r, s) -> dict: full activity y (D18-7).
    comps(d, y, s) -> dict pos -> list[(process identifier, component c)]: valid process-by-process decomposition of the write law (D18-1).
    post(d_new, d_old) -> dict: declared post-processing (e.g. clipping, generation of existence positions); the identity if none is declared.
    resid(r, y) -> r': residue law; with no residue, r is always None.
    """
    def __init__(self, positions, read, comps, post=None, resid=None):
        self.positions = list(positions)
        self.read = read
        self.comps = comps
        self.post = post or (lambda dn, do: dn)
        self.resid = resid or (lambda r, y: r)

def step(host, d, r, s, eps=F(0), sem="raw"):
    """One beat of the run (run equation). sem = 'raw': original-mechanism semantics, all components booked; 'booked': booked-as-effective semantics, only |c| > eps booked.
    Returns (y, d', r', record); the record contains the components at each position, Act⁰, Act^ε, Aff⁰ and Aff^ε."""
    y = host.read(d, r, s)
    cs = host.comps(d, y, s)
    dn = dict(d)
    for x, lst in cs.items():
        if sem == "raw":
            dn[x] = d[x] + sum(c for _, c in lst)
        else:
            dn[x] = d[x] + sum(c for _, c in lst if abs(c) > eps)
    dn = host.post(dn, d)
    rn = host.resid(r, y)
    act0 = {x for x, lst in cs.items() if any(c != 0 for _, c in lst)}
    acte = {x for x, lst in cs.items() if any(abs(c) > eps for _, c in lst)}
    aff0 = {x for x in host.positions if dn.get(x, F(0)) != d.get(x, F(0))}
    affe = {x for x in host.positions if abs(dn.get(x, F(0)) - d.get(x, F(0))) > eps}
    return y, dn, rn, {"comps": cs, "Act0": act0, "Acte": acte, "Aff0": aff0, "Affe": affe}

def run(host, d, r, hist, eps=F(0), sem="raw"):
    ys = []
    for s in hist:
        y, d, r, _ = step(host, d, r, s, eps, sem)
        ys.append(y)
    return ys, d, r

def potential(host, d, r, signals):
    """Tendency Φ_σ (D18-6): the full-activity response over the labeled signal class."""
    return {s: host.read(d, r, s) for s in signals}

def exact_dyn_same(host, s1, s2, alphabet, L, eps=F(0), sem="raw"):
    """Finite verification of exact dynamical identity (D18-14) over all histories of length ≤ L; returns (whether identical, number of histories).
    eps and sem apply to both the runs and the comparison: the default is original-mechanism semantics (sem = "raw"); booked-as-effective semantics must be passed explicitly as sem = "booked" together with ε."""
    n = 0
    for k in range(L + 1):
        for h in product(alphabet, repeat=k):
            n += 1
            y1, _, _ = run(host, s1[0], s1[1], h, eps, sem)
            y2, _, _ = run(host, s2[0], s2[1], h, eps, sem)
            if y1 != y2:
                return False, n
    return True, n

def booked_sum(lst, eps):
    """Component sum of the purely numerical filtered write W^ε_num: counts only components with |c| > ε (this is the operator of sem = "booked" in step)."""
    return sum(c for _, c in lst if abs(c) > eps)

def booked_sum_F(lst, eps, cert):
    """Component sum of the F-layer certified filtered write W^ε_F: counts only components with |c| > ε and cert_eff = 1 ([S2] 4.2). cert: {process: 1/0/'⊥'}, default 1."""
    return sum(c for rho, c in lst if abs(c) > eps and cert.get(rho, 1) == 1)

def pairing_ok(cs_a, cs_b, pi):
    """Sufficient condition for process-identity pairing (Theorem 18-2(1), booked-as-effective clause): for every position x, the multiset of (process, component) of state a at x
    equals that of state b at π(x) (values preserved component by component; the same threshold ε on both sides). This guarantees that the purely numerical filtered write W^ε_num commutes;
    the F-layer certified filtered write W^ε_F additionally requires equal certification states on both sides of the pairing (cert_pairing_ok)."""
    keys = set(cs_a) | {k for k in pi}
    for x in keys:
        la = sorted(cs_a.get(x, []), key=lambda t: (str(t[0]), t[1]))
        lb = sorted(cs_b.get(pi.get(x, x), []), key=lambda t: (str(t[0]), t[1]))
        if la != lb:
            return False
    return True

def cert_pairing_ok(cs_a, cert_a, cert_b, pi):
    """The action-certification states on both sides of the pairing are equal: cert_eff of process ρ at position x equals cert_eff of the same-named process at position π(x). cert: {(process, position): value}."""
    for x, lst in cs_a.items():
        for rho, _ in lst:
            if cert_a.get((rho, x), 1) != cert_b.get((rho, pi.get(x, x)), 1):
                return False
    return True

def mat_mul(A, B):
    return [[sum(A[i][k] * B[k][j] for k in range(len(B))) for j in range(len(B[0]))] for i in range(len(A))]

def hidden_forever_lti(A, C, delta):
    """Linear time-invariant closed loop Δ' = AΔ with reading CΔ: by Cayley–Hamilton, checking k = 0..n−1 is the universal criterion (Theorem 18-7(4))."""
    v = [[x] for x in delta]
    for _ in range(len(A)):
        if any(row[0] != 0 for row in mat_mul(C, v)):
            return False
        v = mat_mul(A, v)
    return True

# ---- F-layer compilation and the two readings of the history condition (Theorem 18-4) ----
def f_component(law, s):
    """The three classes of numerical law of [S2] D12: ('const', b) / ('lin', b, k, R) / ('bil', b, k, j1, j2)."""
    if law[0] == "const":
        return law[1]
    if law[0] == "lin":
        _, b, k, R = law
        return b + k * sum(s[j] for j in R)
    _, b, k, j1, j2 = law
    return b + k * s[j1] * s[j2]

def f_supp_act(law, s):
    """Functional support of [S2] D15 (mathematically nonzero, independent of ε)."""
    if law[0] == "const":
        return set()
    if law[0] == "lin":
        return set(law[3]) if law[2] != 0 else set()
    _, b, k, j1, j2 = law
    if k == 0:
        return set()
    out = set()
    if s[j2] != 0:
        out.add(j1)
    if s[j1] != 0:
        out.add(j2)
    return out

def histcond_F(law, s, T, eps, formed):
    """HistCond_F of [S2] D17, D18 (path certification taken as 1, cert_read taken as 1): available positions = the already formed positions in Supp_act ∩ T with s > ε."""
    avail = {i for i in f_supp_act(law, s) & set(T) if s[i] > eps and i in formed}
    return 1 if avail else 0

def histcond_diff(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts):
    """Numeric-difference reading with single-set contrasts: returns 1 if some valid contrast that changes only one formed position in T with depth > ε makes the total component difference > ε, and 0 otherwise.
    This tests the numerical part of [17] Definition 6′(C) on the single-set subprotocol; it is not a complete certificate. contrasts: table of legitimate values {position: [value…]}."""
    base = f_component(law, s)
    pos = [i for i in T if i in formed and s[i] > eps]
    for i in pos:
        for v in contrasts.get(i, []):
            s2 = dict(s); s2[i] = v
            if abs(f_component(law, s2) - base) > eps:
                return 1
    return 0

# ---- HistCond_F with path and read certification; joint contrasts; two columns of raw judgments; F-layer booking (with cert_eff) ----
def histcond_F_full(law, s, T, eps, formed, path_from, cert_read=1):
    """[S2] D18: HistCond_F = [Avail_act ≠ ∅] ∧ cert_read ∧ Path({(i,t) : i ∈ Avail_act}, m).
    path_from(start set) -> 1/0/'⊥': the path status, on the declared evidence-path graph, from that start set to this mechanism's event (D14)."""
    avail = {i for i in f_supp_act(law, s) & set(T) if s[i] > eps and i in formed}
    if not avail:
        return 0
    if cert_read == 0:
        return 0
    p = path_from(frozenset(avail))
    if p == 0:
        return 0
    if cert_read == "⊥" or p == "⊥":
        return "⊥"
    return 1

def histcond_diff_joint(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts):
    """Cartesian-closure enumeration (used when the declared family of jointly valid contrasts is explicitly the Cartesian closure of per-position candidate values): legitimate values are taken simultaneously on any nonempty subset of the formed positions in T with depth > ε.
    Returns 'numeric difference crosses threshold' or 'numeric difference does not cross threshold' (only the numerical part of [17] Definition 6′(C); the latter is not a negation either)."""
    from itertools import combinations, product as prod
    base = f_component(law, s)
    pos = [i for i in T if i in formed and s[i] > eps]
    for k in range(1, len(pos) + 1):
        for sub in combinations(pos, k):
            for vals in prod(*[contrasts.get(i, []) for i in sub]):
                s2 = dict(s)
                for i, v in zip(sub, vals):
                    s2[i] = v
                if abs(f_component(law, s2) - base) > eps:
                    return "numeric difference crosses threshold"
    return "numeric difference does not cross threshold"

def readback_hist(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts, path_from, cert_read=1):
    """Two columns of raw judgments: (value of the F-layer functional support, numeric-difference judgment under joint contrasts)."""
    hf = histcond_F_full(law, s, T, eps, formed, path_from, cert_read)
    hd = histcond_diff_joint(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts)
    return ("functional support established" if hf == 1 else ("functional support undetermined" if hf == "⊥" else "functional support not established")), hd

def f_layer_update(comps, eps):
    """F-layer booking of [S2] 4.2: counts only the components with Act_F = [|c| > ε] ∧ cert_eff = 1. comps: [(c, cert_eff)]."""
    return sum(c for c, ce in comps if abs(c) > eps and ce == 1)

def raw_update(comps):
    return sum(c for c, _ in comps)

# ---- D18 three-valued conjunction; read-back atoms and three-column summary; F-layer contrast under the actual equation ----
def kleene_and(vals):
    """Three-valued conjunction: 0 if any item is 0; ⊥ if no item is 0 but some item is ⊥; 1 if all items are 1."""
    if any(v == 0 for v in vals):
        return 0
    if any(v == "⊥" for v in vals):
        return "⊥"
    return 1

def max_contrast_diff(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts):
    """Maximum difference over the Cartesian closure: use only when the declared family of jointly valid contrasts is exactly the Cartesian closure of per-position candidate values (declared explicitly, never a default)."""
    from itertools import combinations, product as prod
    base = f_component(law, s)
    pos = [i for i in T if i in formed and s[i] > eps]
    best = F(0)
    for k in range(1, len(pos) + 1):
        for sub in combinations(pos, k):
            for vals in prod(*[contrasts.get(i, []) for i in sub]):
                s2 = dict(s)
                for i, v in zip(sub, vals):
                    s2[i] = v
                best = max(best, abs(f_component(law, s2) - base))
    return best

def kleene_or(vals):
    """Three-valued disjunction: 1 if any item is 1; ⊥ if no item is 1 but some item is ⊥; 0 if all items are 0 (or there are none)."""
    if any(v == 1 for v in vals):
        return 1
    if any(v == "⊥" for v in vals):
        return "⊥"
    return 0

def contrast_family(contrasts):
    """Normalized contrast family (preamble of Theorem 18-4).
    contrasts as {position: [value…]}: single-set shorthand; each value at each position forms one single-set contrast, and no joint contrast is generated automatically; the declared coverage is recorded as “complete”.
    contrasts as {"contrast": [...], "coverage": "complete"/"incomplete"}: a frozen set of contrasts, each of the form {"id": name, "set": {position: value}, "evidence": {...}};
      a joint contrast must be listed explicitly as one entry (or generated by a joint-validity predicate and then listed).
    Returns (list of contrasts, coverage)."""
    if "contrast" in contrasts:
        fam = [dict(id=k.get("id", "contrast %d" % n), set=dict(k["set"]), evidence=dict(k.get("evidence", {})))
               for n, k in enumerate(contrasts["contrast"])]
        ids = [k["id"] for k in fam]
        if len(set(ids)) != len(ids):
            raise ValueError("duplicate contrast identity")
        return fam, contrasts.get("coverage", "complete")
    fam = []
    for i in sorted(contrasts, key=str):
        for v in contrasts[i]:
            fam.append(dict(id="%s→%s" % (i, v), set={i: v}, evidence={}))
    return fam, "complete"

def numeric_diff_family(law, s, T, eps, formed, fam, cover):
    """Numeric-difference item: counts only the contrasts that satisfy [17] Definition 6′(B) (changing only formed positions in T with depth > ε).
    Values: “numeric difference crosses threshold”, “numeric difference nonzero, below threshold”, “numeric difference zero”; if none crosses the threshold and the declared coverage is incomplete, records “contrast domain insufficiently covered”."""
    base = f_component(law, s)
    m = F(0)
    for k in fam:
        act_set = {i: v for i, v in k["set"].items() if v != s[i]}
        if all(i in T and i in formed and s[i] > eps for i in act_set):
            s2 = dict(s); s2.update(act_set)
            m = max(m, abs(f_component(law, s2) - base))
    if m > eps:
        return "numeric difference crosses threshold"
    if cover != "complete":
        return "contrast domain insufficiently covered"
    return "numeric difference nonzero, below threshold" if m > 0 else "numeric difference zero"

def numeric_diff(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts):
    """Numeric-difference item: the numerical part of [17] Definition 6′(C), not a complete certificate (the contrast family is normalized by contrast_family)."""
    fam, cover = contrast_family(contrasts)
    return numeric_diff_family(law, s, T, eps, formed, fam, cover)

def readback_atoms(law, s, T, eps, formed, contrasts, path_from, cert_read=1, cert_eff=1, evidence=None,
                   independent=None):
    """Read-back atoms and three-column summary of Theorem 18-4.
    Contrast family: normalized by contrast_family; for each contrast, the difference and the certification are computed on the same beat, the same process, the same target and that contrast's identity, and the disjunction is then taken over complete witnesses;
    certification is bound to the very contrast that yields the threshold-crossing difference and is never stitched together across contrasts.
    Atoms: numerical support Avail, read certification cert_read, path Path, HistCond_F (the original conjunction of [S2] D18), Act_F (with cert_eff),
    numeric difference (numeric_diff_family), the per-contrast details, and the clause-by-clause evidence for the four clauses of [17] Definition 6′ (same-beat, bearing position, route, action).
    evidence: common evidence of the same F event {key: 1/0/'⊥'}, declared as applying to every contrast; a contrast's own “evidence” takes precedence clause by clause. Clause defaults:
    same-beat is given by the construction of the contrast and recorded as 1; bearing position is judged per contrast by clause (B) (1 if only formed positions in T with depth > ε are changed, 0 otherwise); route is bound to the bearing positions that
    the contrast actually changes—1 if the path from these starting points to this event is 1, '⊥' otherwise (a path of 0 does not exclude another material route; a separately declared
    valid actual route must be supplied independently, with its own certificate); the aggregate Path is only an independent atom and does not replace the per-contrast route; action takes Act_F.
    The contrast details record, per contrast, (contrast identity, |Δ|, that contrast's witness value, that contrast's actual four clauses); “Def. 6′ four clauses” takes the four clauses of the witness contrast (of the first contrast if there is no witness contrast).
    “certificate status”: the identity of the witness contrast if there is one; otherwise the value of the disjunction of the listed contrast certificates, with “declared domain not yet covered” added when the declared coverage is incomplete.
    Merging the action clause: the F atom Act_F is the action evidence of the same event.
      Act_F = 0 (explicit refutation): an external claim cannot override it; if the external claim is 1, an “evidence conflict” is recorded and the blockade stands per the original refutation; the clause takes 0.
      Act_F = 1: if the external claim is 0, an “evidence conflict” is likewise recorded and the clause takes 0 (conservative blockade); if the external claim is 1 or absent, the clause takes 1.
      Act_F = ⊥ (unproven): an explicit external certification can supply it (its value is taken, and the source is recorded as “explicit certification”); if the external input is explicitly ⊥, the source is recorded as “explicitly unproven”;
      if nothing is given, the source is recorded as “unproven” and the clause is ⊥.
    independent: a witness from a different mechanism or an independent R-layer witness, which must be {event identity, mode, certification basis, action}; it is listed separately under the atom “independent witness”
      and is not merged into the four clauses, the complete certificate or the summary of this F event; if fields are missing, “independent witness incomplete” is recorded.
    complete certificate = the three-valued disjunction, over the contrasts, of [|Δ| > ε ∧ four clauses]. The summary follows the three-way split of values in [17] Definition 6′: if the complete certificate is 1, it records
    “effective difference certified”; otherwise, if the numeric difference is nonzero and below threshold (with complete declared coverage) or HistCond_F = 1, it records “functional support established”; in all other cases it records
    “effective difference not certified”. The summary does not override the atoms; conflicts, unproven items and refutations are shown in the atoms “evidence conflict” and “action source”, respectively."""
    evidence = dict(evidence or {})
    fam, cover = contrast_family(contrasts)
    avail = {i for i in f_supp_act(law, s) & set(T) if s[i] > eps and i in formed}
    path = path_from(frozenset(avail)) if avail else 0
    hf = histcond_F_full(law, s, T, eps, formed, path_from, cert_read)
    c = f_component(law, s)
    act = kleene_and([1 if abs(c) > eps else 0, cert_eff])
    nd = numeric_diff_family(law, s, T, eps, formed, fam, cover)
    conflicts = []
    ext = evidence.get("action")
    if act == 0:
        act_item, src = 0, "F-atom refutation"
        if ext == 1:
~~~
